## The overkill audit (2026-08-22, from field data)

The owner reported the skill was "not strict enough" while asking whether anything had been
overkilled. Both were true, and the evidence was one real ledger from a production run. The rule
this section exists to record: **a documented field with zero adoption and zero code readers is
not a standard, it is a rumour.**

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CUT `steps`.** The row's numbered-walk array was called "the part that works" and was filled on **0 of 19** rows in the only real run, read by **0** lines of engine code — so Stage 3's walk rule, which was conditioned on it, selected nothing across an entire build | `references/completion-ledger.md` → "`does` is a witness case, not a title" | **REMOVED (field evidence)** | Measured against `a private field repo's `.claude/slices.json``. `does` plus `evidence` carried the whole run; the requirement survives as "a witness case with a concrete input and an observable outcome", which is one field instead of two. |
| **CUT the five-value `from` enum**, keep `discovered: true` | `references/completion-ledger.md` → "that one bit is what the scope delta reads" | **REMOVED (field evidence)** | Never written once. The extractor read only `from`, so the delta printed `discovered +0` on a run that discovered two rows and traced them to production. The field wrote `discovered: true`; the code now reads both and believes either. |
| **CUT the per-slice `irreversible` array** | — | **REMOVED (field evidence)** | 0 of 4 slices, no code reader, and the axis is already owned by **compound-v:make-it-stable**. The one-way-door interrupt stays: it is prose and needs no field. |
| **CUT four of Stage 5's five paragraphs**, and the stage itself | `references/mep-gate.md` → "Discharge before the landing commit" | **REMOVED** | Only `discharge` was adopted in the field (voluntarily, on 8 of 14 passed rows). The DORA/28M-workflow opener, "merging is not releasing", and the refusal list produced nothing; discharge folds into Reckon and the spine returns to four stages. |
| **CUT a "does this row grade the run rather than the product" heuristic**, written and removed the same hour | — | **REMOVED (falsified)** | It flagged two field rows — *"each claim is backed by a real read-back"* and *"anything not done is stated as not done"* — that were closed with concrete evidence naming real record ids. A quantifier scoped to a **named artifact** is walkable; only one scoped to the run itself is not, and no cheap test separates them. Do not re-add the regex. |
| **ADDED — the per-capability floor.** A capability with open rows and no passing row did not ship, whatever the aggregate says | `get-shit-done` → "The floor" | **PRIMARY (field-observed)** | A mid-run snapshot of the same ledger read 84% with one of four capabilities at **0 of 3** passed. Corroborated upstream: gsd-core's changelog records "a confirmed incident closed a phase with 6/30 plans unexecuted, including its entire final scope", fixed with a per-unit refusal rather than a better aggregate. Two exclusions are earned, not cosmetic: an all-dropped slice was cut, and a blocked-only slice must not wedge the run. |
| **ADDED — typed drops.** `void` owes nothing; `moved` must name a successor row present in the ledger | `get-shit-done` → "how a capability quietly empties out" | **PRIMARY (field-observed)** | The natural experiment is in the same ledger: the two drops that named successors left healthy capabilities, and the one that named none (*"founder steer: reply in DMs"*) emptied its capability while the percentage stayed green. An **untyped** drop only warns — requiring the field outright would brick every ledger written before it existed. |
| **ADOPTED from the field: `ask` and `notSlices`.** The owner invented both because the skill stated the rules in prose and gave them no key | `get-shit-done-1-carve` → "notSlices" | **PRIMARY (field-invented)** | Something invented in the field is evidence of a gap; something documented and unused is evidence of bloat. This pass found three of the second and two of the first. |

## Recon economics and the shape rule (2026-08-23)

The owner's read: the corpus exists for one job — find the simplest implementation that is also
effective and scalable — and the kit was over-spending on the mining. Measured against this kit's
own sweeps, he is right, and the cause is not the one it looks like.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Question breadth, not lane coverage, is what makes recon expensive.** Across four sweeps in one session — 17 agents, ~2.04M subagent tokens, 523 tool calls — the cheapest (2 lanes, ~248k, one narrow question anchored to a real ledger) produced everything that shipped, while the most expensive (7 agents, ~926k, an open question) returned findings its own critic scored as roughly one-third restatement | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "it is the question, not the number of lanes" | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | Instrumented from this session's own workflow runs; the restatement estimate is the critic agent's, not a recount. Recorded because it reconciles two rules that look opposed: **coverage stays mandatory** (a lane at zero is a defect) while **depth is bought with the slice's confidence**, and neither is the cost driver. Re-measure before quoting the figures — they are four runs, not a study. |
| **The shape must pass simple + effective + scalable at once**, and the one that gets dropped is scalable, which is why "simplest" is not "smallest" | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "Simple" | **JUDGMENT-CALL** | The owner's own framing, adopted as the recon acceptance test. The mechanism climb it feeds is **compound-v:simplest-thing-that-works**, which already owns it and needed no re-derivation. |
| **The spine never routed to the kit's most-used skill.** `simplest-thing-that-works` fired 127 times in 90 days — more than any other skill in its group — while `get-shit-done` mentioned it zero times, so nothing in the pipeline ever made the architecture call | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "compound-v:simplest-thing-that-works" | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | `bash scripts/skills-audit.sh`, which counts real Skill invocations in local transcripts. The skill was already reachable by handoff (its description says "or another Compound V skill hands off here") — the spine simply never handed off. That is why recon returned reports instead of decisions. |
| **Distillation, not indexing.** Shape-plus-trap pairs are written down only when the shape will recur, in a table kept short enough to stay true | `references/shapes.md` → "A recon earns a row here only when the shape will recur" | **JUDGMENT-CALL, respecting a standing refusal** | A corpus index, embedding layer, persisted route-map and memo-cache of findings are all on `workflow-investigation`'s explicit do-not-re-propose list, one of them with a measurement: a production findings-cache scored **0 hits in 133 attempts**. An index over a growing corpus rots while `grep -n` over it is current by construction. The curated table is the form that already works in this kit — the canonical-exemplar table, whose own rule is that most stacks have no row and that is the rule working. |

## The shape table (2026-08-23)

10 arrangement-plus-trap pairs in `searching-patterns` (was eight at first writing; the count is corrected here rather than left to drift). The **shapes** are mostly public,
long-named patterns; the **traps** are the distilled part and the reason the table exists at all.
Three research lanes returned 16 candidates and independently reached the same placement verdict —
apart from the machinery ladder, because they are orthogonal to it — so the ladder in
`simplest-thing-that-works` gained only a pointer and the three-way test.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Frozen prefix, variance at the tail** — putting the date or a flag in the system prompt invalidates the whole cache on every call | `references/shapes.md` → "Frozen prefix, variance at the tail" | **PRIMARY** | Armin Ronacher, *Agent Design Is Still Hard* — https://lucumr.pocoo.org/2025/9/29/agent-design/ . The one shape in the table with a clean public primary for both halves. |
| **Sticky routing makes a routing mistake permanent** rather than a 1-in-N blip | — | **PRIMARY (considered, not adopted)** | Anthropic, *A postmortem of three recent issues* — https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/a-postmortem-of-three-recent-issues . Cut from the table for relevance, not for grounding; keep the row so the shape is not re-proposed as unsourced. |
| **The other six traps** — cursor expiry making full-resync a day-one path; a job id absorbing nothing as an idempotency key; derived data outliving a deleted source; a reconciler having no memory of intent; the deleted human reviewer having been the real test suite; a check the author can read being one the author can satisfy | `searching-patterns` → "Its trap" | **DISTILLED (private corpus) — deliberately uncited** | Each was observed in a shipped product or a practitioner's own account inside the local teardown and transcript corpora. The **shapes** are public patterns under their usual names (transactional mirror, idempotency key, bitemporal append-only, desired-state reconciler, fleet migration); the **traps** are what the corpus adds, and they ship as the kit's own distillation because `scripts/check.sh` forbids citing an internal path from a shipped file — correctly, since those paths mean nothing to anyone installing this. Treat them as experience, not as measurement: re-derive any one before betting a design on it. |
| **The table is capped on purpose.** 16 candidates surfaced; 6 were adopted, and 2 of the 4 pre-existing seed rows were cut | `references/shapes.md` → "A recon earns a row here only when the shape will recur" | **JUDGMENT-CALL** | The two cut seeds described this kit's own ledger rather than an architecture, which is the failure mode a shape table drifts into: it starts documenting the tool instead of the world. A dozen curated pairs beat a thousand retrievable ones, and the cap is what keeps it true — the same rule the canonical-exemplar table above runs on. |

## Recon lands in the ledger or it did not happen (2026-08-23)

The decomposition diagnostic recorded above — *when a sequence of steps collectively underperforms a
human even though each individual step performs well, the decomposition itself is the bug* — was run
against this kit for the first time. **The decomposition is not the bug; one stage is.**

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Stages 1, 3 and 4 are load-bearing; stage 2 produces nothing that survives the run.** In the only field ledger that exists, all six slice keys are written on 4/4 slices, `does` on 27/27 rows, `evidence` and `discharge` on 24/24 passed rows, and the run closed **24 passed / 3 dropped / 0 blocked / 0 todo** — while `shape`, `trap` and `delete` appear **zero** times | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "The output is a decision, not a report — and it lands in `slices.json` or it did not happen" | **PRIMARY (field-observed)** | `a private field repo's `.claude/slices.json``, 4 slices / 27 rows. **N=1 — this is one run, not a rate.** The fix is a slot, not a paragraph: stage 2 was asked for a report and given nowhere to put it, so its output died with the session and the next project re-mined it. |
| **The depth-by-confidence rule ran inverted in the field.** `force` was written on both slices at confidence `0.9` and on neither at `0.65` | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "Both failures are live and opposite" | **PRIMARY (field-observed)** | Same ledger. This is why the budget shipped as a table keyed on the `confidence` stage 1 already writes, rather than as more prose: over-researching what you know and skipping what you don't is one mistake with two faces, and only the first was previously named. |
| **The new fields have a reader, which is what the two cut fields lacked.** `ledger-extract.jq` warns when a slice below `0.7` confidence carries no `shape`, and when a `shape` carries no `trap` | `references/completion-ledger.md` → "And they have a reader" | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | Verified against the real ledger: it flags **S2 and S4**, exactly the two `0.65` slices, while `ok` stays true and `open` stays 0 so a finished run is never wedged. Advisory by design, for the same reason untyped drops warn. The bar was set by `steps` and the `from` enum, both specified here and both cut for being written zero times — *one field that gets written beats two where the second is a rule nobody enforces.* |
| **The shape table is a cache with a miss path, not an index.** A hit is the whole of recon for that slice; a miss buys one hunt scoped to that one domain, at build time | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "Look the shape up before you research it" | **JUDGMENT-CALL (the owner's, adopted)** | His call, chosen over both a standing mining sweep and a pre-loaded corpus index. It respects `workflow-investigation`'s standing refusal (a production findings-cache measured 0 hits in 133 attempts) while giving the table the only growth path that does not re-mine: graduation at Reckon, when a shape will recur. |
| **Honest cost of this pass:** stage 2 went 608 → 657 words and the skill 3997 → 4156, against a check.sh warning it already tripped | — | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | `bash scripts/check.sh`. Recorded rather than rounded away: two duplications were cut (the simple/effective/scalable test, which `simplest-thing-that-works` owns verbatim, and the three-channel enumeration, which `references/prior-art.md` carries) and a 4-row budget table plus the cache mechanism were added. **Net +49 words is a cost, not a saving** — the mechanism is the return on it, and the file is now further past the compaction head. |

---

| **The filter existed and never reached the workers.** `prior-art.md` already carried three channels, the X-retrieval reality and a strict source bar — all read by the orchestrator, none of it inherited by a dispatched agent | `references/prior-art.md` → "The dispatch block" | **PRIMARY (self-observed, this session)** | Four workers dispatched over this corpus with scope and budget but no retrieval method hit the 600s watchdog and returned **nothing**; re-dispatched with a 15-call cap and a no-file-over-5,000-lines rule, two returned 19 findings of which **19/19 quotes relocated**. **The stall cause is NOT established and the first version of this row overclaimed it** — a later trio carrying the full block, ceiling included, stalled as well, so the ceiling is a defensible precaution rather than a demonstrated fix. What the block demonstrably buys is the *quality* of what returns: every worker that finished under it returned relocatable quotes and an honest UNCOVERED list. The fix is a block pasted per dispatch, not an index: the owner's call is explicitly against indexing because the corpus grows most days, and `grep -n` over it is current by construction while an index rots. |

## The 100% bar, and the class where "nothing is impossible" breaks (2026-08-23)

Two of the owner's claims, checked inline against the primary essays after three background attempts
stalled. Both survive in a sharpened form; neither survives as stated.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **100% is over a declared denominator, never over every axis** — and the second target provably does not ship | `get-shit-done-4-reckon` → "100% is over a declared denominator" | **PRIMARY** | Yan et al., *What We've Learned From a Year of Building with LLMs* — against a requirements list running reliability, harmlessness, factual consistency, usefulness, scalability, cost, security, privacy and fairness: *"If we try to tackle all these requirements at once, we're never going to ship anything"*, and *"We have to accept that the first version won't be perfect, and just launch and iterate"* (both `articles/what-we-ve-learned-from-a-year-of-building-with-llms.md:440`). **This does not weaken the owner's bar; it is what makes it reachable.** The kit already had the mechanism — declared rows, `notSlices`, typed drops — and never said the distinction out loud, so "100%" was readable as "every axis at maximum", which is 80% forever by construction with the missing fifth *undeclared* rather than unbuilt. |
| **The tractability claim holds for building and fails for effectiveness** | — | **PRIMARY** | Same essay, opening line: *"creating products and systems that are effective—beyond a demo—remains deceptively difficult"* (`:13`). Recorded without an anchor because `get-shit-done` already carries the mechanism twice — Steinhardt's fake-the-hard-part ceiling in stage 1, and the declared hit band in stage 3. The claim needed no new prose, which is itself the finding. |
| **The named class where more effort does not clear the ceiling: the invisible asymptote** | `get-shit-done` → red flags, "Round after round of engineering and the metric will not move" | **PRIMARY** | Eugene Wei, *Invisible Asymptotes* — *"a ceiling that our growth curve would bump its head against if we continued down our current path"* (`articles/invisible-asymptotes.md:31`). Some ceilings belong to the path rather than to the effort; the move is a different path, named out loud, not another round. This is the one genuine limit on *nothing is impossible*, and it is a routing signal rather than a reason to stop. |
| **Cost of this pass, stated rather than rounded away:** `get-shit-done` 3,997 → 4,425 words | — | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | `bash scripts/check.sh`. Four mechanisms were added (recon slots, the confidence budget, the cache miss-path, the declared-denominator rule) and two duplications cut; one deliberate compression pass over the ledger section returned only **33 words**, because that section was already dense and further cuts would have removed executable content. The file sits past the compaction-head warning it already tripped, and the remaining fix is **structural — move a section to a reference — not more word-shaving.** Flagged for the owner rather than decided here. |

## The first CONNECTION, and it caught a defect in the same day's work (2026-08-23)

The connection step was added to `references/prior-art.md` and immediately run against this session's
own output. It returned one, and it was a real defect in the recon budget shipped hours earlier.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A recon budget keyed on a pre-investigation `confidence` is keyed on the one signal this kit forbids, read at the one moment it is least knowable** | `get-shit-done-2-recon` → "The band is a pre-investigation guess" | **CONNECTION (two sources, stated by neither)** | **Rests on:** (a) `designing-agents` — *"a worker cannot reliably tell when it doesn't know — so the trigger must be an observable external signal ... never self-assessed confidence"*, which rules self-assessment out everywhere else in the kit; (b) Scott Wu, Cognition, on routing for coding agents — *"The initial agent prompt isn't enough to know the difficulty of the task"*, and *"you can't know until you've actually investigated the code"* (https://twitter.com/ScottWu46/status/2071718682250928421). **Neither states the conclusion:** (a) never mentions the recon budget, (b) never mentions this kit. The fix keeps the band as the opening allocation and adds two observable signals that force a re-band — a shape-table miss on a ~0.9 slice, and a first read contradicting the slice's `unknown` — with the revision legal only **upward**, so it cannot become an exit. |
| **The connection step earned its place on its first run** | `references/prior-art.md` → "Then read your sources against each other" | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | One connection, one real defect, in the same pass — consistent with the local elaboration eval that returned 18 novel connections and six defects from reading note pairs together. Warrant for the step: a model recalls a pre-trained relation at **99% in context** and **"absolutely zero"** on held-out reversals, fine-tuning below chance, so the implied inference does not exist until something writes it down. **This is not a licence to build a graph** — concept-mapping lost to retrieval practice at *d* = 1.50 with its advocates' confidence measured as inverted. Read together, write it down, keep the pointers sparse. |

## Guidelines vs process steps — the form that transfers (2026-08-23)

The owner's thesis: a founder-CTO's judgment can be ported into agents from the corpus, so that
acquiring it personally is not the highest-ROI path. The corpus answers the *form* question directly,
and the answer constrains this kit rather than licensing more of it.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Output-quality guidelines beat process steps, measured.** A prompt moved **2.32 → 7.86** with a list of qualities the output should have; replacing them with a list of process steps scored **7.3 — lower** — and the author reverted | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "describe what a good answer looks like, do not script how to reach it" | **PRIMARY (local corpus, verified)** | A local operating spec for agentic search, §8 — built 2026-08-20 from ~40 grounded investigations over 41 transcript files (487,340 lines), several hundred product write-ups and the live `openai/codex` tree, with weaker rules marked `[ASSERTED]`. Its four load-bearing transcript citations were relocated at their exact line numbers during this pass. The spec is local, so it cannot be cited from a shipped file — the rule ships as the kit's own and this row is the trace. |
| **Scaffolding value is a function of post-training and decays toward zero**: ~73% of a base model's gap recovered, **~7%** of a post-trained model's; and *"the reasoning path is ALREADY in the output space of pretrained models — it just doesn't rank first under greedy decoding"* | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "do not script how to reach it" | **PRIMARY (local corpus, verified)** | Same section. The strongest counter-example is reconciled rather than dismissed: Ng's outline→search→draft→critique→revise scaffold reports 48.1% → 95.1% on HumanEval, but on GPT-3.5 in 2024 — the base rung — and by 2025 the same author is *"ripping out scaffolding and letting the LLM do more."* **Both are true; scaffold value is model-conditional and must be re-measured per model, not inherited.** |
| **The decomposition diagnostic** — when a sequence of steps collectively underperforms a human even though each individual step performs well, the decomposition itself is the bug | — | **PRIMARY (local corpus, verified)** | Same section. Recorded here without an anchor because it is a standing test against **this kit**, not a rule inside it: `get-shit-done` is a staged pipeline, and this is the check that says when staging is the defect rather than the fix. The mechanisms it ships — a Stop hook, a coverage command, an on-disk ledger — are exempt from the critique by construction, because they change what the agent *can* do rather than instructing it. The prose that scripts is not exempt. |
| **Model the founder-CTO — but the strong form ("knows more than big tech") is refuted, and porting the CONCLUSION is the failure** | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "Who to model" | **PRIMARY (corrects a prior JUDGMENT-CALL)** | The owner's framing was adopted whole; an unbiased pass over 8 primary essays keeps the weak form and cuts the strong one. **Refuted:** knowledge produced by operating volume cannot be manufactured below it (Character.AI, *“we serve more than 20,000 inference queries per second”*), the founder heuristic has a scale expiry rather than being a superset (Anthropic interpretability), and unknown-unknowns accrue from long operation (*“for shiny new technology the magnitude of unknown unknowns is significantly larger”*, McKinley). **Survivorship refuted:** Etsy reached its IPO while it *“went several years without shipping anything at all”* (McKinley), and *Founder Mode* openly samples *“a lot of the most successful”* founders and argues management, not architecture. **What survives, and it is the load-bearing half:** port the rationale-generating method, never the settled answer — *“Bad strategies state a policy without explanation, which decouples them from the context they were made”* (Will Larson). **This is the primary-source warrant for making `trap` mandatory alongside `shape`:** a shape without its trap is precisely a decontextualised policy, so the pair is not a stylistic preference but the difference between strategy and its bad kind. Also note the premise slip: the generalist-under-constraint posture is already big-lab practice, so "big tech = narrow specialist" does not hold either. |
| **The rung-7 gate is structural (write topology), not strategic (moat)** — and the ladder stays adaptive all the way up | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "Adaptive, never capped" | **PRIMARY (corrects a prior JUDGMENT-CALL)** | The earlier row read "climb to rungs 6–7 for the moat"; the rung-7 half is **anti-correlated** with the evidence and is now cut. **Attribution correction:** the owner's claim was *adaptive machinery — simplest to revolutionary, never capped* — not "multi-agent is the moat mechanism", and this finding was first recorded against a claim he did not make. The anti-underkill rule was never in question; only the admitting test for rung 7 was. Two primaries converge on a *structural* gate instead. Cognition, *Don't Build Multi-Agents*: *"Actions carry implicit decisions, and conflicting decisions carry bad results"*, escalated to *"I would argue that Principles 1 & 2 are so critical, and so rarely worth violating, that you should by default rule out any agent architectures that don't abide by them"* — a sentence that refuses exactly the "but it is the moat" carve-out. Anthropic, *How we built our multi-agent research system*, reports the 90.2% win and bounds it on the same line: *"some domains that require all agents to share the same context or involve many dependencies between agents are not a good fit for multi-agent systems today"*, with value appearing only as a budget gate against ~15× tokens. Cognition ten months later converges on the write property: *"multi-agent systems work best today when writes stay single-threaded and the additional agents contribute intelligence rather than actions"*. **The sign error:** a moat is usually a coherence asset — one taste, one set of implicit decisions — which is precisely what dispersed writers destroy, while the demonstrated wins are read-only support machinery. The kit already stated the correct rule twice (`designing-agents` → "parallelize intelligence, keep writes single-threaded"; `dispatching-parallel-agents` → same, with the 90.2% caveats); this row closes a third file that contradicted them. |

## The founder-CTO pass (2026-08-23) — 9 principles in, 3 adopted

A local field manual for founder-CTOs carries nine architecture principles. Mining them against the
kit produced mostly **duplication**, which is the finding worth recording: the kit already owns most
of this, and adding it under new words would dilute retrieval rather than deepen it.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Split the decider from the executor** — a big model sketches, a small fast one performs the mechanical edit using the existing artifact as draft tokens, reported at ~13× on the execution half | `references/shapes.md` → "Split the decider from the executor" | **PRIMARY (local field manual) + kit-supplied trap** | The shape is public — it is the apply-model pattern shipped in at least one production editor. **The source names no trap, so one was supplied rather than invented:** actions carry implicit decisions (Walden Yan, Cognition — https://cognition.ai/blog/dont-build-multi-agents), so the split holds only where the execution is genuinely mechanical. Recorded because a shape shipped without a trap is the exact thing this table refuses. |
| **Production agents run on the order of 100:1 input:output**, so cache behaviour dominates the bill far more than output length | `references/shapes.md` → "100:1 input:output" | **PRIMARY (local field manual)** | Merged into the existing frozen-prefix row rather than shipped as a second shape — it is the *reason* that shape is a cost lever, not a separate arrangement. |
| **Detection is the enabling condition for fallback, not the reporting layer**, and the ladder is graded (full model → a simpler model on two or three trusted features → a couple of if-statements → a constant) rather than binary | `make-it-stable` → "detection is the enabling condition" | **PRIMARY (local field manual)** | The skill already had fail-over and graceful degradation but assumed the failure was known. The silent case — confidently wrong rather than absent — never trips a 500, and that is the case the ladder exists for. |
| **REFUSED as already owned — do not re-add under new words:** harness-beats-model (same model, different harness, a measured multi-point swing); build for the capability ~18 months out and delete the crutches on release; taste as the moat; design-doc-kills-the-project-early; founder-mode-stay-in-the-details | — | **DECLINED (duplication)** | `architecting-ai-systems` owns the first two explicitly, including "what plumbing to throw away on the next model release". `product-taste` owns taste. The design-doc point is Steinhardt's de-risk ordering, already in `get-shit-done`'s carve. `founder-mode` is not a shape. Six of nine principles were already in the kit — recorded so a later pass does not read the same manual and re-import them. |
| **Two independent sources converge on the same warning**: a harness encodes stale assumptions about the model's limits, so a capability jump turns yesterday's workaround into dead weight — and separately, *"prompt scaffolding is rented, and the lease is short"* | — | **PRIMARY (corroboration)** | The field manual reaches it from the architecture side (context-management scaffolding became liability when the model stopped needing it); the agentic-search spec reaches it from the prompting side. Neither cites the other. Convergence from two directions is the strongest signal available for a rule nobody can A/B cheaply. |

| **The form rule, promoted to the authoring constitution** — default to qualities, script only where the failure is measured | `references/skill-format.md` → "Default to qualities; script only where the failure is measured" | **PRIMARY (local corpus, verified)** | Same agentic-search spec §8 as the row above. Promoted here because it governs every future skill edit rather than one skill's content, which is the compounding form of the finding: a rule inside a skill improves that skill, a rule in the constitution improves the next twenty. It also supplies the standing test against this kit — the decomposition diagnostic — which is why `get-shit-done`'s Stage 3 became a set of closing conditions rather than a numbered sequence. |
| **The audit that followed it** — every paragraph of `get-shit-done` checked against "name the measured failure this prevents" | — | **JUDGMENT-CALL (recorded outcome)** | Result: two genuine duplications cut (three quotes doing one job; an engine paragraph restating the hook's own header), and **nothing else**. Each remaining paragraph names a documented failure or a quality, so the skill sits at ~4100 words against a 3750 advisory and stays there. The word-budget row has now been dropped three times with the reason stated; further cutting would delete grounded rules, which is a worse defect than being over an advisory. Recorded so the next pass does not re-run the same audit and reach the same place. |

## The X channel, measured twice (2026-08-23)

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The unit to extract is the outbound link, not the post** — 66 corpus-seeded accounts, 1,081 original posts, 166 links the corpus lacked, **71 essay-shaped (~7% of posts)**; the rest were product pages, release notes, jobs, events, gists and a Google Form | `references/prior-art.md` → "The unit to extract is the outbound link" | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | Harvested via a commercial X read API's last-tweets endpoint, taking `entities.urls[].expanded_url`, deduped against the owner's local essay corpus (190 articles, 155 distinct authors). The candidate list stays local; only the measurement ships. Re-measure before quoting — one harvest, one day. |
| **Engagement over-selects announcements in DIRECTION but not in MAGNITUDE.** 66-account roster: **7.8x mean / 6.8x median**, 9 of the top 10. Broader 120-account roster: **2.7x / 3.2x**, 3 of the top 10. Yield stable at **6.6% and 6.1%** of posts | `references/prior-art.md` → "Engagement over-selects announcements" | **PRIMARY (self-measured, two harvests)** | The first harvest's magnitude was written up here as the finding and a second harvest corrected it: the multiplier tracks how exec-heavy the roster is, since a founder's most-liked post is a launch and a researcher's is not. Quote the direction, never the constant. A third measurement in the same session was **invalidated by its own author** — announcements had been moved into the upstream filter, so the test measured n=0 and reported no inversion; it was rerun with the original classifier. |
| **5 of 72 candidate handles returned no data and were dropped without a guess** | — | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | Recorded because it is the self-correcting property that makes a hand-built roster safe: an unverified handle returns nothing rather than someone else's feed, so a wrong guess costs a row, never a fabricated attribution. |

## The owner's three untested claims, checked against the essay corpus (2026-08-23)

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Port-vs-learn HOLDS, with a hard condition: you must retain discrimination.** *"It produces motion that works, but feels mediocre, and if you can't tell the difference, you'll ship it"* | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "the corpus cannot carry discrimination" | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated)** | Emil Kowalski, *Train Your Judgement* — https://emilkowal.ski/ui/train-your-judgement . Corroborated by the year-of-building-with-LLMs authors, who *"focus on the data, looking at inputs and outputs for several hours a week"* — the practitioners do not delegate the looking. The claim's ROI argument survives for **generation** and fails for **grading**; the fix is a stated check, an author-blind grader, or a named human, not more reading. |
| **"Nothing is impossible" HOLDS for buildability and FAILS on effectiveness** — *"creating products and systems that are effective—beyond a demo—remains deceptively difficult"* | `get-shit-done` → "Buildability is rarely the constraint" | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated)** | *What We've Learned From A Year of Building with LLMs*. The named class where the claim breaks is not "hard to build" but "hard to make effective past the demo" — which is what stages 3 and 4 already target, so this sharpened the opener rather than adding machinery. |
| **"100% not 80%" is NOT in conflict with shipping early — the conflict dissolves on the definition of done.** *"Instead of defining success as shipping a feature, the capability funnel breaks down AI performance into progressive levels of utility"* | — | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated) — ALREADY COVERED, deliberately not ported** | Hamel Husain, *A Field Guide to Rapidly Improving AI Products*, with the failure it prevents: committing to a date the technology cannot meet means you *"either ship something subpar to hit the deadline or miss the deadline entirely. Either way, trust erodes."* The kit already carries this as the **hit band** declared before the build plus the MEP bar; re-adding it would have been the duplication this session exists to cut. Recorded so the next pass does not re-derive it. |

## Shape table row 10 — the completeness watermark (2026-08-23)

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A mirror must expose the boundary of what is final, or consumers read provisional data as settled** | `references/shapes.md` → "Expose the completeness watermark" | **PRIMARY (two independent sources converging)** | (1) Field-observed in the owner's own production audit: a third-party ads API holds spend provisional for three days, the display window ran to today, and a flat account rendered **$750 where the truth was $1,750** — with a second-order failure where gating only the prior window made a flat series show a permanent decline. (2) A teardown of an unrelated system reaches the same shape from the enrichment side: the pipeline's `analyzed` field is *"the **watermark a replica must expose** — it is the field that tells a consumer whether the AI fields on this row are final."* Two domains, one shape, so it cleared the recur bar; the existing mirror-and-cursor row covers cursor expiry and event-filter projection and does **not** cover the provisional tail. |
| **The hunt that produced it was anchored to a live build, not a sweep** | — | **JUDGMENT-CALL (method note)** | This is the per-domain miss-path running for real: one narrow question against a named project, two greps, four files opened. ## The owner's four claims, tested against the essay corpus (2026-08-23)

Four read-only agents, one wave, each briefed cold with `prior-art.md`'s dispatch block pasted
verbatim. 190 primary essays. **43 of 45 quotes relocated positionally**; one was transcription
drift (a curly apostrophe for a straight one, quote real at the cited line) and one returned nothing
on a full-file grep and was dropped as fabricated rather than repaired.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Port judgment rather than acquire it — the porting half confirms, the "rather than" is refuted.** Taste demonstrably ports: *"Human taste is captured once, then enforced continuously on every line of code"* (an engineer shipping 1M LOC with zero hand-written lines). But every successful port in the corpus was performed **by people who already held the judgment**, and paid to extract it — one team spent *"every Friday (20% of the week)"* detecting slop before any of it could be encoded. Two mechanisms explain why the shortcut fails: verifying code is near-symmetric with writing it, so directing never gets cheap; and the agent never accumulates judgment across sessions, so the human's copy is the only one that compounds | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "a hard ceiling on porting" | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated)** | The corpus contains **no** case of judgment ported by an operator who lacked it. Partial rescue in the other direction: most of what "senior" denotes is org-navigation a solo founder with agents does not need. Honest gap — no source addresses the actual arithmetic of hours-to-acquire vs hours-to-port; every ROI statement is mechanism, not measurement. |
| **The founder-CTO is the model to port — holds only in the weak form already shipped.** What survives is operate-what-you-shipped and the anti-over-engineering reflex. What fails is every premise offered for it: the exit does not certify the architecture, judgment is *"transferable across similar domains, but not across different domains"*, self-attributed success is named as the marker of **weak** judgment, and scale-only failure classes are unreachable from below | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "Who to model — and the strong form of this is wrong" | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated)** | The skill's existing correction was independently reproduced by an agent that had never seen it, down to the same McKinley quote. Sharpest disconfirmation: the corpus's one essay directly on *what ports into a coding agent* is by a platform specialist and the answer is structural code review — an altitude skill, not a career shape. |
| **"Nothing is impossible; there is always a simple implementation" — fails as stated, holds inside a nameable boundary.** The class where it breaks is **verification-poor and tail-specified**: the cheap verifier saturates before the problem does, so the simple version self-certifies. Three markers travel together — the metric tops out, the simplification *is* the semantics (it got simple by narrowing the input distribution), and the residual is not refactorable because you cannot build a key for what you cannot describe | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "Name the class where" | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated)** | Three builder accounts of teams who could not: a voice model whose naturalness scored level with real speech *until conversational context was restored*; a retrieval team's *"We missed. Again."*; and an incident whose clean root-cause fix exposed a deeper bug in an **approximate** top-k. Two are frontier labs writing about their own shipped systems. The tell is the addition: state the check that would catch you being wrong on the real distribution — if your only verifier is one the simple version already passes, you are inside the class. |
| **Multi-agent for the moat — fails; the axis is wrong, and the kit had already corrected it.** Every primary source separates wins from losses on **write topology and verifiability**, never on strategic importance: *"multi-agent systems work best today when writes stay single-threaded and the additional agents contribute intelligence rather than actions."* The demos that make multi-agent look ready for ambitious work *"share a property most real software doesn't: a simple, verifiable success criterion"* | `simplest-thing-that-works` → "the gate on the top rung is structural, not strategic" | **PRIMARY (corpus, relocated)** | A moat is high-value but taste-laden and write-heavy — it clears the value condition and fails verifiability and write-topology, so the selector routes multi-agent toward search, review and audit, which is the "simple tasks" the claim excluded. The pattern that actually shipped is the dumbest one available: a review loop that works *because* the agents *"do not share any context beforehand."* |
| **The fan-out confirmed rather than discovered, and that is the predicted result.** Three of the four corrections were already in the skills before the agents ran; the marginal yield of ~450k subagent tokens was **one** new class plus independent primary-source backing for three existing positions | — | **PRIMARY (self-measured)** | Recorded because it is this kit's own recon economics measured on itself a second time: an open question over a good corpus returns mostly restatement. The confirmation was not worthless — three positions moved from reasoned to sourced, and one fabricated quote was caught by the relocation check — but it is confirmation, and a run that had *needed* those answers would have been four agents late. The cheap move remains asking the narrow question first and checking what is already written down. |

| **The check reads the goal's ground truth, and the ground truth differs by kind of goal** — a behaviour goal by a run, a replication goal by comparison to its source, a property goal by an adversarial probe, a removal goal by absence, a migration by parity | `get-shit-done-1-carve` → "what would show the goal UNMET" | **JUDGMENT-CALL (generalised from a field failure)** | First caught on a replication task: a run closed with **135 rows passed and every page wrong**, because each row was a true statement about the target and none was a comparison to the source. **The first draft of this rule was written as a rule about references, which was overfitting to the instance** — the owner caught it. The general form is the falsifiability question *what would show this goal unmet*, which adapts per goal type; the mechanism (`is_check: true`, one row per slice carrying the slice's own check) was already general and did not change. Recorded because the overfit version is the tempting one: a vivid failure makes its own shape look like the rule. |

## Subagent-driven development — the theory tested, and mostly falsified (2026-08-23)

I shipped "one slice, one fresh context; serial dispatch buys the whole benefit" and then had three
lanes hunt for falsification rather than confirmation. They found it. The rule was rewritten the same
day; this row exists so the appealing version is not re-shipped.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The tail-skimp is real and the fix is an externalised checklist, not a fresh agent.** An agent identified **20 call sites**, changed **5**, and stopped — and what shipped for it was an in-context to-do tool | `get-shit-done-3-build` → "20 call sites" | **PRIMARY (local corpus)** | The decisive finding: an externalised row set reads the same to a long context and a fresh one, so a fresh context stops the checklist being *re-derived* but is not why it works. The ledger was already the right mechanism; dispatch was about to be bolted on top of it for a problem it does not solve. |
| **Delegation has a fixed per-handoff cost and lost to a single agent below a very large reading volume**; at same-model there is no cheaper-worker arbitrage, so isolation is all it can buy | `dispatching-parallel-agents` → "billed twice" | **PRIMARY (local corpus)** | The measurement was run with a *frontier orchestrator plus cheap workers*, so cost arbitrage was working in delegation's favour and it still lost below threshold. Same-model dispatch is therefore strictly worse on cost — a point the number alone understates. |
| **STRUCK 2026-08-30 — shipped unsourced, do not restore.** "A subagent-reaching harness scored below the same model run natively" | — | **RETRACTED** | An adversarial recheck could not locate a primary for it anywhere: it appeared only in this file and in the skill text, citing nothing. Uncheckable claims are worse than absent ones because they read as grounded. The companion claim — ~13% gained by deleting a manager-worker layer — is real but was **mis-scoped**: the deleted layer was an LLM planner that chose its own decomposition and re-planned after each subtask, so it bears on model-chosen fan-out and says nothing about a human-fixed carve. Both removed from the skill. |
| **Primacy is a property of the causal mask, not of long conversations** — position alone moves retrieval by >20% on i.i.d. data with no data bias | `dispatching-parallel-agents` → "relocates" | **MEASURED (local corpus)** | This is the finding that survives against *both* positions: splitting does not escape tail-neglect, it concentrates it in the orchestrator, which is the one context nobody refreshes. |
| **A deterministic orchestrator costs something too** — replacing a reasoning orchestrator with fixed round-robin dispatch cost ~6 points on one benchmark | `dispatching-parallel-agents` → "cost ~6 points" | **MEASURED (teardown)** | Routing is only half an orchestrator's job; detecting a stalled or looping worker is the other half, and a shell script cannot do it. This kit's substitute is the three-strikes `blocked` rule, which files the stall rather than re-planning around it. |
| **Where fan-out pays hugely (57% → 96.5%), the partition was fixed by a human in advance and executed deterministically**; model-chosen fan-out landed at or below one long context at several times the tokens | `dispatching-parallel-agents` → "fixed by a human in advance" | **PRIMARY (published benchmark)** | This is the finding that *rescues* the spine's shape rather than the theory: slices written before the build are a pre-specified partition, and `ledger.sh` is a deterministic executor. The win is attributable to the partition, not to delegation. |
| **Workers cannot be trusted with shared state even serially** — a shipped serial one-agent-per-subtask pipeline force-marks completion because the coder agent does not reliably update the plan file it was told to update | `get-shit-done-3-build` → "grading its own homework" | **PRIMARY (teardown)** | Independent confirmation of the one rule from the original paragraph that survived: the worker returns evidence, the orchestrator flips the row. |

| **Dispatch the CHECK, not the build** — a clean-context reviewer finds ~2 real bugs per PR on code the same system wrote, ~58% of them severe, and works best precisely because it shares no context with the author | `get-shit-done-3-build` → "Dispatch the check, not the build" | **MEASURED** | Walden Yan / Cognition, *Multi-agents: what's actually working* — https://cognition.ai/blog/dont-build-multi-agents . The one dispatch in the corpus with a measured payoff, and it is on the verification half. Every measured result on dispatching the *build* runs the other way, including a workshop agent that went 62% → ~92% by **deleting** two of three sub-agents and replacing them with primitives. |
| **The condition that decides it: dispatch a slice only where the orchestrator can re-run that slice's check itself, without the worker's trace** | `get-shit-done-3-build` → "without the worker's trace" | **JUDGMENT-CALL (from an adversarial recheck)** | Where a row can be confirmed only by the walk the worker performed, the split bought context isolation at the price of an unauditable ledger — the orchestrator grades a walk it never saw, and the single-writer rule buys *bookkeeping* integrity, not *verification* integrity. This is the rule that settles a question two prior passes answered in opposite directions, and it is checkable before dispatching rather than after. |
| **Under dispatch, the ledger becomes the only carrier of cross-slice knowledge** — and in the kit's one field run the fields that would carry it (`shape`, `trap`, `delete`) were written **zero** times across four slices | `get-shit-done-3-build` → "has no carrier at all" | **MEASURED (field)** | Under a single context a shape survives in the conversation anyway; under dispatch it does not. So dispatch makes correctness depend on fields nobody wrote — a precondition to check *before* splitting, not a consequence to discover after. |
| **Two prior conclusions on this question were both wrong, in opposite directions** | — | **PROCESS RECORD** | First pass: "one slice, one fresh context; serial dispatch buys the whole benefit." Second: "mostly wrong, the ledger is the fix." The second over-corrected by using **cost** evidence against a **quality** claim, and by filing the strongest supporting finding (a human-fixed partition run by a deterministic executor going 57% → 96.5%) as a caution when it describes this kit's own shape. Recorded because the pattern — a confident reversal that is itself an overshoot — cost two commits and was only caught because the owner pushed back on his own experience. |

## Measured negative: description-keyword overlap does not predict whether a skill fires

**Built, measured against ground truth, and deleted the same session (2026-09-01).** Recording it
here so the next person does not rebuild it.

The idea was reasonable: `scripts/trigger-eval.sh` spends a live CLI turn per fixture, and a full
92-row run exhausted a session quota mid-suite leaving 56 fixtures unmeasured. An instrument you
cannot afford to run does not run. So a free static pre-check — score each fixture's vocabulary
against the description that should match it, flag the low ones — looked like the obvious complement.

**It does not work, and the failure is not a tuning problem.** Scored over 89 fixtures, it flagged 15
skills below 25% overlap. Cross-referencing against the live eval that had actually run:
**13 of those 15 demonstrably fire correctly** — an ~87% false-positive rate. `council` scored LOW on
both its fixtures and fired **3/3** on the real CLI, including *"nobody can agree on this and theres
no test that would tell us who's right"*.

**Why, and why no threshold rescues it.** Descriptions match on **intent**, not keywords — the router
says so in its own text — so lexical overlap is measuring a proxy that is genuinely uncorrelated with
the target here. Lowering the threshold hides the false positives without adding signal; raising it
flags everything. The instrument is aimed at the wrong quantity.

**The damage it would have done is specific:** it would have sent an editor to rewrite 13 working
descriptions, in a kit whose descriptions are the always-loaded surface that decides whether anything
fires at all. A cheap instrument that points confidently at healthy tissue is worse than no
instrument, because it gets acted on.

**What remains true:** the quota problem is real and unsolved. The live eval is still the only thing
that measures firing, and it must be run in slices (`bash scripts/trigger-eval.sh <skill>`) rather
than as a 92-case sweep. The eval now distinguishes a quota refusal (`NOT RUN`) from a routing
failure, because pooling them produced a summary reading "33 ok, 56 failed" that invited exactly the
wrong conclusion.

## Practitioner-corpus pass (2026-08-30) — Lenny's Podcast + the Karpathy guidelines

Twelve additions mined from ~115 long-form practitioner interviews and one public skills repo, each
put through an adversarial verifier that defaulted to reject. Seventeen candidates were raised; six
died (already owned by a sibling skill, or the transcript did not support the claim once read) and
these survived. **Caption caveat that applies to every Lenny row below:** the sources are YouTube
auto-captions with no speaker labels and heavy proper-noun garbling, so attribution to a named guest
is only made where the episode's own deixis settles it. Where it does not, the note says "a speaker."
Timings are not cited because caption line numbers are not stable across re-transcription.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Gate the closure, not the call** — any data an agent can reach its user can make it leak, any action it can take its user can make it take, in any order; a per-call gate is defeated by chaining, and a fresh instance is context isolation, not a security boundary | `agent-security` → "Gate the closure, not the call" | PRIMARY | Sander Schulhoff (HackAPrompt), Lenny's Podcast — "Why securing AI is harder than anyone expected and guardrails are failing" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J9982NLmTXg . He states the composition rule directly ("requests that are individually legitimate can be illegitimate in combination") and reads the split-request pattern off the first largely-agent-run intrusion campaign. The skill deliberately does not name the vendor or a CVE: the episode is a spoken account, not an incident report, and the mechanism is what transfers. |
| **Agency is an axis separate from machinery**; stage autonomy draft → direct → consequential tools, promote on information gain (operators stop editing), demote on a model swap or adjacent-task drift | `designing-agents` → "Agency is a second axis" | PRIMARY | Two AI consultants, Lenny's Podcast — "Why most AI products fail: Lessons from 50+ AI deployments at OpenAI, Google & Amazon" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z7T1pCxgvlA . The episode has two guests whose surnames the captions destroy, and it carries no speaker labels, so no individual attribution is made. The load-bearing mechanism — the operator's accept/edit/reject log *is* the error analysis, harvested while error is still cheap — is stated outright, as is the named failure (jumping straight to full autonomy, which fails in a hundred uncatalogued ways and reads as "the approach doesn't work"). The loan-application example is theirs. |
| **Inference spent on free usage is acquisition spend, not a margin leak** — booked as margin the gradient is *minimize*, booked as acquisition it is *spend to the payback floor*; and it is reach, not conversion | `founder-distribution` → "That remedy assumes serving is free" | PRIMARY (single operator, structural inference) | Head of growth at Lovable, Lenny's Podcast — "The new AI growth playbook for 2026 | How Lovable hit $200M ARR in one year" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6qAB6aUMIeA . The captions render the guest's surname three different ways, so no name is used in the skill. **Warrant is deliberately hedged in the skill text**: one operator, one company, an exploding category, and a small remaining cost base. The free-generation-to-activated-user rate is *not* published by anyone — the skill says so and tells the reader to measure their own. |
| **Who you ask ≠ whose asks you take** — take the *main benefit* from the very-disappointed, take the *roadmap* from the somewhat-disappointed who cite that same benefit, discard the rest; delivery method resets the survey baseline alongside wording and segment | `founder-distribution` → "Who you ask and whose asks you take" | PRIMARY (one operator's own procedure, no control) | Rahul Vohra, Lenny's Podcast — "Superhuman's secret to success" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0igjSRZyX-w . Corrects a live misreading of the existing very-disappointed paragraph, which as written invites sourcing the roadmap from the lovers — the one move he explicitly rules out. Warrant grade is stated in the skill line itself, per this file's own convention for uncontrolled operator accounts. |
| **Bound the upside before you build the measurement rig** — when the maximum achievable difference is worth less than the cost of measuring it, the experiment is a guaranteed loser however cheap the code is | `startup-taste` → "Bound the upside before you build the apparatus" | PRIMARY | Stewart Butterfield, Lenny's Podcast — "Mental models for building products people love" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLe-zy5r0Mk . The Slack threads @-prefill case is his: cut pre-release, returned six months later defended as research-backed, and the research was **2.17 vs 2.14** messages per thread. **The "not even clearing significance" hedge is his recollection, not a reported statistic** — the skill carries it as recollection and must keep doing so. His prior question ("*why* would a longer thread be better? A shorter one may be") is the transferable half. |
| **Most failures found in error analysis deserve a prompt sentence, not a judge**; a judge is a meta-eval you must align before trusting, and the loop lands near **4–7 judge prompts per product** | `evals` → "Most failures you find deserve one more sentence in the prompt" | PRIMARY (mechanism) + JUDGMENT-CALL (the 4–7 knob) | Hamel Husain & Shreya Shankar, Lenny's Podcast — "Why AI evals are the hottest new skill for product builders" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BsWxPI9UM4c . The 4–7 count is attributed in-episode to Shreya as a number from experience, so it is a practitioner default, not a measured threshold — the skill states it as a calibration signal ("read a number far above it as a prompt you never edited"), never as a gate, and explicitly distinguishes failure-*modes* judged from the independent axes one deliverable is scored on, so it does not contradict the skill's own five-binaries example. Consistent with this file's existing precedent for 30–100 traces and 25–50 alignment examples. |
| **Re-categorise from the notes after editing the taxonomy, and give the categoriser a literal "none of the above"** — edited labels invalidate the first pass's assignments, and a categoriser with no escape hatch forces notes into the nearest bin, producing a clean-looking count | `evals` → "none of the above" | PRIMARY | Same episode (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BsWxPI9UM4c), from the live screen-shared walkthrough. Speaker not resolvable from the captions at that point — recorded as "a speaker." Also carries the axial-coding bar: a bucket like "capability limitations" names no fixable thing; the standard is *specific and actionable*. |
| **Undo cannot un-deliver** — for an action whose effect lands on other people, a confirmation earns its keep, and it should spend itself on **disclosing the blast radius** rather than on a bare yes/no | `product-taste` → "Undo only works while the effect is still inside your system" | PRIMARY | Stewart Butterfield, same episode (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kLe-zy5r0Mk), recounting Slack's `@everyone` warning: copy stating the recipient count and time zones, plus a confirm; usage dropped off immediately, and he credits it with teaching users what `@everyone` cost. **The "147 people in eight time zones" figure is his illustrative rendering of the copy, not a quoted product string** — the skill uses it as an example of the shape, which is how it must stay. Narrows, and does not overturn, the skill's existing prefer-undo-over-confirm rule. |
| **The first artifact that looks real becomes the baseline** — generation is linear (one prompt, one direction), so open design calls start at 3–4 unstyled layout-block alternatives, and a promising one gets tabled before it gets refined | `product-taste` → "Render to judge a property, not to choose a direction" | PRIMARY | Bob Baxley, Lenny's Podcast — "35 years of product design wisdom from Apple, Disney, Pinterest and beyond" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=X-83gvgVaWc ("the first mark on the canvas, after which everything you do is in response to that mark"; block frames, on which "people couldn't comment on what it looked like, so the discussion had to be conceptual"). Independently corroborated in the same corpus by Jenny Wen, head of design for Claude — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eh8bcBIAAFo — on throwing eight to ten directions at the wall and curating, against coding tools being "super-linear." Two independent sources is why this shipped as a section rather than a line. |
| **Trace a value to what writes it before you calibrate on it** — a metric, an `is_fraud` column, a README benchmark or a green badge is a claim by whatever emitted it; when the chain bottoms out in "presumably someone, somewhere," it carries no weight | `critical-thinking` → "Trace a value to what writes it" | PRIMARY | Tom Verrilli, CPO of Whatnot, Lenny's Podcast — "This CPO regrets that product management exists" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ruvis-VWg2s . His ladder (*the dataset says fraud* → *labelled in the dataset* → *labelled by ops* → *nobody has read the SOP*) is the worked example; the skill leads with the code instance so the org anecdote reads as illustration. Slots under the existing Calibrate/Weakest-joint moves because it gates the *inputs* those moves consume. |
| **Derive sub-check bars from the top-level check and write the composition line** — bars picked seam by seam don't add up (four stages at 90% ⇒ ~66% end to end), and a tree with no statable composition is diagnostic only | `frame-the-goal` → "Derive each sub-check's bar" | PRIMARY (the failure) + CANONICAL (the arithmetic) | Matt LeMay, Lenny's Podcast — "The one question that saves product careers" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ts9ZvlkeWGs : his consulting test is to ask a team to show the arithmetic from the bottom goals to the top, and he reports never having seen those layers form a traceable trail. The compounding arithmetic itself needs no citation (it is multiplication, and `evals` already owns the agent-trial form). **This edit also rewrote the skill's worked example**, which previously wrote every bar as the same `≥ X%` placeholder and so violated the rule the section now states. |
| **Clean up the orphans your change created; leave pre-existing dead code alone** — the asymmetry is the rule, and a drive-by deletion is an unreviewed change riding on an unrelated batch | `batched-implementation` → "Deleting pre-existing dead code" | PRIMARY | Andrej Karpathy's public observations on LLM coding pitfalls — models "don't clean up dead code" and "still sometimes change/remove comments and code they don't sufficiently understand as side effects, even if orthogonal to the task" — https://x.com/karpathy/status/2015883857489522876 , packaged as https://github.com/multica-ai/andrej-karpathy-skills . Filed as a red-flag row because it is a *discipline* failure (the model knows the rule and skips it), which is the form `references/skill-format.md` prescribes for that failure type. **The other three Karpathy principles were checked and deliberately NOT adopted** — Think Before Coding is already carried, more deeply, by `brainstorming` (one question at a time, 2–3 approaches) and `frame-the-goal` ("guessing intent well is part of the job, and guessing *silently* is the failure"); Simplicity First by `simplest-thing-that-works` and the router's overkill law; Goal-Driven Execution is `frame-the-goal`'s entire thesis plus `verification-before-completion`. Re-adding them would be duplication, not grounding. |

Ten more, from episodes the first pass never opened. Same adversarial gate (13 raised, 2 died). Same
caption caveat as the block above: auto-captions, no speaker labels, garbled proper nouns —
attribution only where the episode's own deixis settles it, and **several rows below deliberately
de-name the company**, citing the mechanism instead, per this file's convention that a named vendor
invites not-my-scale discounting while the mechanism transfers.

The supply-blocker class (a credential, access) is kept explicit because a proposal is meaningless there, and `STATE.md`'s template was updated in the same edit so the two documents can't drift. |

Landed in `mep-gate.md` rather than the SKILL because `get-shit-done` has no word budget left (below). |
| **Size a layer by its share of the loop before engineering it** — driving a step to zero removes at most that step's share; and once the model writes the output the time moves onto the human's review-and-accept surface, which a better model makes *bigger*, not smaller | `architecting-ai-systems` → "size it by its share of the loop" | PRIMARY | An agentic-IDE founder, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5Z0RCxDZdrE — running Amdahl's Law against the "AI writes 90% of the code" inference: 30-of-100 units spent writing, cut to 3, lands at 73 — a 27% win, not 10×; their measured gain was 30–40%. **Corrected before shipping:** the miner's "caps the gain at 1/(1−share)" is a *speedup ratio* and contradicted the 27% time-reduction beside it; the skill says "removes at most that share".
