| **What you admit into the index is an architecture decision upstream of the retrieval shape** — an unfiltered corpus mixes asserted opinion with validated outcome and the model weights them alike; curate by validation status and attach the outcome | `architecting-ai-systems` → "not an ingestion detail" | PRIMARY | A large professional-network platform's CPO, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R-zCfLQD_84 — on internal agents: the corpus work outweighed the dedicated build work, and a first attempt that handed the agents everything on the drive "hallucinate[d] like crazy". **Two constitution fixes applied:** the source's month-scale calendar estimate was replaced by the ratio (skill-format forbids week/month estimates, and the ratio is the decision-relevant part); and "a small golden set" was reworded to "curate by validation status" because `evals` already owns *golden set* for a different object. |
| **Backtest a watcher on what was found *late*** — a reviewer that only reproduces the concerns your process already raises has proved nothing; replay an artifact whose post-hoc history you hold and score it on the late-discovered subset alone | `ai-system-reliability` → "backtest a watcher before you trust it" | PRIMARY (single operator, no control) | Same episode — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R-zCfLQD_84 . They validated an in-house trust reviewer by re-running an already-shipped feature spec through it, and it surfaced both what the team caught up front and the holes found only later. **The reject-if-it-only-reproduces-the-up-front-findings bar is the kit's derivation, not the source's stated policy** — kept as an inference, not attributed. |
| **A question asked twice is a signal about the design, not a failure of your explanation** — repeating the answer with more detail never reaches the concern under it; go get the concern instead | `brainstorming` → "asked twice" | PRIMARY (two independent sources) | Wes Kao, Lenny's Podcast — "Become a better communicator" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BIvVGhy_VxU (the question-behind-the-question move), corroborated by Jessica Fain — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RP4vJeIb7WU (get more information on why they think it matters). The probing script leads with "what are you worried about here?" *before* the acceptance condition, because a user can answer an acceptance question in full without ever naming the worry — which is the exact miss the rule exists to prevent. |
| **Ask once whether a hard constraint is actually firm** — when an inherited constraint is the only thing ruling out the approach you'd recommend, name it and what lifting it buys, rather than silently narrowing or declaring the ask infeasible | `brainstorming` → "actually firm" | PRIMARY | Jessica Fain, Lenny's Podcast — "The art of influence" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RP4vJeIb7WU . **Provenance disclosure:** the "ask once, then design inside it" bound is grafted from that episode's trust section rather than its constraint-lifting section; it is carried because without it the rule becomes a scope-widening prompt, and the source does attach its own counterweight (is this the highest-value move?). |
| **A carve-out to the animation gate, granted by the surface's job** — where a surface exists to elicit contribution and its failure mode is silence, personality is the function; substitute a falsifiable test and never port charm to an instrumental surface | `product-taste` → "One carve-out, and the surface's job grants it" | PRIMARY | Dylan Field, Lenny's Podcast — "Why AI makes design, craft, and quality the new moat for startups" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WyJV6VwEGA8 : FigJam judged "built but soulless," *fun* chosen as the differentiator, cursor chat out of the resulting sprint — and Field draws the transfer limit himself (most of it doesn't belong in Figma Design, whose job is to stay out of your way). **Corrected before shipping:** the miner recast gate #3's "state change" as *the user deciding to speak*, which would make #3 unfalsifiable and gut the refusal template; the shipped text suspends #3 and substitutes an explicit test, and states that checks 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 hold unchanged. |
| **Under time pressure cut elements, not quality** — delete whole elements while the slice stays useful, stop at the row that carries the goal, and let the first complaint rank what returns | `get-shit-done-3-build` → "elements, not quality" | PRIMARY (single operator, uncontrolled) | Gaurav Misra (Captions), Lenny's Podcast — "How to win in the AI era: Ship weekly, embrace technical debt, ruthlessly cut scope" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PDobJV8wh1g . Ported into the ledger's own grammar: the stopping rule anchors to the `is_check` row (the skill's name for the row that cannot pass while the goal is missed) rather than the untestable "until removing anything else makes it useless", and every cut becomes a `dropped`/`moved` row so the deletion is a ledger event rather than silent forgetting. The source's condition is carried by the adjacent hit-band bullet, which already excises irreversible writes, money movement, data loss and stranger-facing surfaces to `make-it-stable`. **Budget note: this addition was funded by compressing the Stage 2 corpus-lane paragraph, and `get-shit-done` now sits at 4996 words against the 5000 hard ceiling — see the ceiling row below.** |
| **A fast human preference vote measures presentation** — the cheapest ways to climb a public preference leaderboard are heavier bolding, more emoji and triple length, and they win on fully hallucinated answers; so a human verdict outranks the grader only where the rater did the work | `evals` → "A fast preference vote is a formatting vote" | PRIMARY | Edwin Chen (Surge AI), Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dduQeaqmpnI . "In one training-data vendor's own data" is his in-episode framing, **not a published study** — the hedge must stay. His stated remedy is the expert-rater half, which is why it holds the default slot; the presentation-neutralisation fallback (strip formatting, cap length) is an inference from the measured cause and is explicitly conditioned ("where you can't"). |
| **`git` is not the whole change ledger, and `git bisect` pins a commit only when the cause is in the history** — externally-borne state is live at every commit tested, so every build comes back bad and bisect silently names the oldest commit in the range | `systematic-debugging` → "git is not the whole ledger" | PRIMARY (the ship-rate half) + CANONICAL (the bisect half) | Sebastian Barrios, Lenny's Podcast — "18k engineers, 30k deploys a day" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=puY_dxVKEwU — for the composition of production changes (PRs, config, database updates, some automatic); the skill de-names the company. The host's "more than one deploy per developer" arithmetic is **not** used. The bisect behaviour needs no citation: it is a reproducible property of git (clean history + externally-held bad state + an untested good end ⇒ the oldest commit in the range is reported as first-bad, exit 0, no warning). **Corrected before shipping:** the miner listed "a lockfile re-resolve" among the bisect-invisible causes — a committed lockfile *is* visible to bisect, so it became "a floating dependency that re-resolved". |
| **`get-shit-done` is at its ceiling, and its done-gate already sits past the compaction line** | `get-shit-done` | **PROCESS RECORD / CLOSED 2026-09-21** (next row) | Measured this pass: the file is **4996 / 5000 words** (hard ceiling), and cumulative word counts put **Stage 4 — the MEP done-gate, the skill's whole point — beginning at word 3920**, past the ~3750-word mark where compaction stops re-attaching a skill's tail. So the gate most likely to be needed at hour six is the part most likely to be missing then. Two of this pass's three `get-shit-done` candidates were routed to `references/mep-gate.md` for exactly this reason. **This is a structural fix (split the spine, or move Stage 4 above the line), not an enhancement — it is recorded here as open rather than done, and no further prose may be added to this SKILL without a matching cut.** |
| **CLOSED — the spine is split into an entry skill and four stage skills, and no stage body was reworded** | `get-shit-done` → "The stages — four skills, invoked in order" | **PROCESS RECORD** | 2026-09-21. `get-shit-done` (4,862 words) became the entry — units, ledger, stage routing, red flags — plus `get-shit-done-1-carve`, `-2-recon`, `-3-build` and `-4-reckon`, each small enough to come back whole after compaction (`scripts/check.sh` prints the live counts). A script proved the split lossless: all 145 original non-blank lines verbatim, in order, each exactly once; the only new text is routing — the stage list, one header and one next-stage line per stage. Each wiring clause answers a named upstream failure. **By exact name through the Skill tool**, because the listing always carries every name even when it drops descriptions (code.claude.com/docs/en/skills, rev. 2026-09-17). **Flat in the main conversation, never inside a subagent** — gsd-core froze on nested Task chains, and a depth-1 stage lost its Agent tool so its replan loop produced nothing (open-gsd/gsd-core `CHANGELOG.md`@v1.14.0). **Routed by what `slices.json` shows, never by a pointer** — gsd-core's `current_phase` advanced past half-executed work and resume skipped it (`gsd-core/workflows/next.md:92-94`@v1.14.0). **The next stage named at the top of each stage, not only at its end** — spec-kit found a mandatory step placed after "report completion" skipped, with a zero-match debug log as evidence (github/spec-kit #2688, fix PR #2713). **Re-invoke after compaction** — the harness re-attaches each invoked skill's first 5,000 tokens within 25,000 in total and drops the oldest first, and the entry is the oldest (code.claude.com/docs/en/context-window, rev. 2026-09-18). The cost is four descriptions on a listing already over budget, which is why each is one short sentence; gsd-core hit the same wall at 66 skills and moved to routers that `Read` stage files by path — rejected here, because it gives up invocation by name. **Measured the same day** with `claude plugin eval` (CLI 2.1.278, a local suite, with-plugin arm only): an explicit "use get shit done…" ran the entry then stage 1 in 3/3; seeded ledgers resumed at the right stage — recon 3/3, reckon 3/3, build 2/3 (the third went to `handoff` and ran out of turns); and the natural project prompt fired `using-compound-v` then `startup-taste` in 5/5 on both the split kit and the pre-split HEAD, so first-hop routing did not move. Not measured: whether a stage *does* its job better or worse than the same text did as a section — `scripts/trigger-fixtures.tsv` carries three routing rows per stage. |

### Third pass — the fifteen untouched skills (2026-08-30)

Skill-first rather than episode-first: passes 1–2 read episodes and asked what they offered, which
left half the kit untouched. This pass assigned each unenhanced skill a lane and let it search the
whole corpus by concept, re-reading episodes the earlier passes had walked past. Thirty candidates;
the verifiers rejected two and the chair cut five more — **four of those five were the same idea
proposed into two different skills**, a duplication a per-candidate verifier structurally cannot see,
and one (style-goes-in-an-exemplar-not-the-instruction-file) had already been rejected in pass 2 and
returned.

**Corrected diagnosis (this row was first shipped wrong).** The initial reading was that a 28-of-30
pass rate meant the gate had softened. It had not. Every verifier was told to grep all 30 skills, and
every one answered correctly: a duplicate's twin was a *pending candidate*, not committed text, and a
resurrection had been rejected precisely so it would not be in the files — so "is this new?" honestly
returned yes in all five cases. Nothing was too lenient, and tightening the verifiers would not have
caught any of it. A per-item gate is asked *is this true?*, which a duplicate passes twice; the
question that catches it can only be asked over the pooled set. The high pass rate has a benign
explanation too — these lanes targeted skills with no prior additions, where "already owned" was rare
by construction. Recorded because the wrong mechanism is the more expensive error: it points at the
verifier, which is not where the fix is (`dispatching-parallel-agents` → "no worker can answer it").

Same caption caveat as the blocks above.

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Escalate a one-way-door diff as an acceptance check, not a reading assignment** — name the two or three behaviours a person should exercise and what each should do | `code-review` → "not a reading assignment" | PRIMARY | Mike Krieger, Lenny's Podcast — "Anthropic's CPO on what comes next" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DKrBGOFs0GY . The team building their coding agent with that agent replaced line-by-line PR review with a second agent's review plus a human role that "shifts to acceptance testing," on two findings: the model is generally right, and it opens PRs larger than most people will read. **Two scope notes the skill keeps:** he hedges "pros and cons" and names an imaginable failure, so the skill borrows the *shape* of the escalation and never endorses dropping human review; and "gone well so far" is a snapshot, so no continuity beyond the recording is claimed. |
| **A convention finding backed only by a standard written nowhere in the repo should leave that standard behind it** — propose the one-line rule and the file it belongs in, never write it (the review stays read-only) | `code-review` → "should leave that standard behind it" | PRIMARY | Fiona Fung, Lenny's Podcast — "What happens after coding is solved?" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ybrl4FYM57c . The mechanism is that a model given a framework is good at validating against one, so a judgement stated once in the repo stops being re-derived every review. The drift caveat ("propose it only where someone will keep it current") is the kit's own condition, not hers. |
| **Contestability is a second discard filter for a council chair** — a recommendation whose opposite no competent person would defend carries no information, however true | `council` → "Relevance is one filter; contestability is the other." | PRIMARY (scope-limited) | Nabeel S. Qureshi, Lenny's Podcast — "How Palantir built the ultimate founder factory" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xQkSenlJvwA , the murder-board segment: new joiners kept writing principles like *move fast*, which nobody can reasonably disagree with, and a real principle is one somebody reacts to as wrong. **Scope condition, do not drop it:** the source's test governs *principles* — normative picks from a menu. It does not license discarding a concrete factual defect that happens to be uncontested, which is why the skill line says it tests the proposition, not the members. |
| **Redundant fan-out — run the same task N ways and discard N−1 — is a third legitimate parallel shape**, and the only one in which a coupled single-file task parallelizes; it needs per-run isolation, differently-seeded runs, and a selection budget fixed before you look | `dispatching-parallel-agents` → "Redundant fan-out: run it N ways, keep one" | PRIMARY | A vibe-coding engineer at an AI app-builder, Lenny's Podcast — "The rise of the professional vibe coder" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0XNkUdzxiZI . **Corrected before shipping:** the miner's rationale "writers can't collide when only one survives" is *false* for agent dispatch — discarding losers afterwards does nothing about collision during generation — and shipping it would have produced the exact silent-clobber this skill exists to prevent. Replaced with an explicit isolation condition reusing the skill's own `isolation: "worktree"` vocabulary. The correlated-errors warrant for differently-seeded runs is routed to `designing-agents`, which already owns it. |
| **The scars are the spec** — a contract recovered only from entry surfaces is a clean lie; hunt the anomalous defensive branches and resolve each with `git log -S` / blame rather than judgment | `extracting-specs` → "The scars are the spec" | PRIMARY | Dhanji R. Prasanna, Lenny's Podcast — "How Block is becoming the most AI-native enterprise in the world" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JMeXWVw0r3E , on what a rewrite loses: the accumulated small fixes each added after an incident and never written down. Scoped in the skill to the files step 3 already opened, not a fresh repo sweep. |
| **Deadness is out-of-band** — a dead duplicate path compiles, has tests, and reads exactly like the live one, so reading more code only re-confirms it exists; get usage evidence outside the code and record it with the evidence attached | `extracting-specs` → "Deadness is out-of-band" | GROUNDED-ANALOGY | Two AI consultants, Lenny's Podcast — "Why most AI products fail" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z7T1pCxgvlA . Their example is a support taxonomy where an agent has no basis for choosing among duplicated branches while a human recognises the node last updated in 2019 as dead; the stated remedy is that such rules must be supplied as context rather than inferred. **Transferred** from ticket-routing to code spec-mining, and graded as an analogy for that reason. **Corrected:** the miner's absolute "the code cannot tell you a path is dead" is false where a `@deprecated` marker or an unregistered route does say so; and a fallback to an `uncertainty` note was added, without which the item licenses manufacturing a deadness Invariant — regressing the skill's own honesty rule. |
| **Green says nothing about which default is live** — enumerate the toggles a branch introduced and state each one's shipping value before landing; aim to land at most one | `finishing` → "Green says nothing about which *default* is live" | PRIMARY | Rippling's chief product officer, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O_W76LR77Vw . An internal app shipped to its sole administrator and opened on an empty screen; the cause was a feature flag nobody disabled, and his standing image is the shim a contractor walls into a house. The single-flag-at-ship standard is one he explicitly calls extreme and possibly unachievable — hence "aim to" in the skill line. Captions render his surname three ways, so no person or company is named. |
| **Sweep the state file before deleting it** — sort each `Do not` and evidence entry into what dies with the goal and what is a durable fact about the repo, and promote the latter into the agent-readable instruction file in the same commit | `handoff` → "Sweep before you delete" | PRIMARY | Alexander Imbiricos, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z1ISq9Ty4Cg . Closes a leak this skill created itself: it names `Do not` as the one thing git cannot recover and then deletes it. |
| **Enumerate failure modes before pricing them, and split irrecoverable from recoverable** — with an accumulation goal for the recoverable class, because stacked recoverable failures add up to an irrecoverable one | `make-it-stable` → "stacked recoverable failures add up to an irrecoverable one" | PRIMARY | Fiona Fung, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ybrl4FYM57c ("bad versus sad"). **Corrected:** the miner's opening contradicted the paragraph two lines above it (which already prices the failure) and conflated an irrecoverable *failure* with an irreversible *action*; and "moves 1–4 in full" reimposed the fixed liturgy the host section explicitly disowns. The trailing clause — write the acceptability line beside the raw number, since a bare latency or error rate does not say whether the surface is healthy — comes from the same passage and is carried as guidance, not as a measured finding. |
| **A resource that only accumulates fails on a date, not on a call — and near its ceiling it impersonates a transient fault**, so the classify step never sees it coming; report headroom and an exhaustion date, not a retry policy | `make-it-stable` → "Near a ceiling → stop retrying and compute the exhaustion date" | PRIMARY (founder's hedged recollection) | Ivan Zhao, Lenny's Podcast — "Notion's lost years, near collapse during COVID" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IIPKMixTMfE . One vertically-scaled Postgres instance ridden to the largest machine that existed, weeks from full shutdown by his own hedged account, with three-to-six months between starting the clock and running out. The "weeks" figure is recollection on a podcast, not an audited number, and the skill says so. **A second candidate making the same claim was cut as a duplicate.** |
| **A label naming a set or quantity is a parameter, not a caption** — it sets a default that has to land inside the range the mechanism needs, and the *translated* string is what sets it per locale | `product-taste` → "is a parameter, not a caption" | PRIMARY | Robby Stein, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kOnsqqVbIeY , recounting Instagram's Close Friends: shipped as *Favorites*, people added two or three, and where the string was mistranslated as *best friend* many added exactly one — at which point the reply loop the feature exists to close cannot close. |
| **A new kind of thing gets its own surface, not a slot in the old container** — the standard is *coherent but different*; bending the existing container to fit it is the worse version of the same mistake | `product-taste` → "coherent but different" | PRIMARY | Same episode — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kOnsqqVbIeY . Stories shared the app's look, took camera-roll uploads and was shareable in DM while being visibly its own surface; the earlier failed attempt made the *core feed* ephemeral. |
| **Step 0 of a review: if the change has an observable surface, drive it before you read the diff** — a model of the change assembled from the code reads that code as its own spec, so correct code for the wrong result is invisible from the inside | `recheck` → "drive it before you read the diff" | PRIMARY (two independent sources) | Zevi Arnovitz, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1em64iUFt3U (manual QA first, then `/review`), independently corroborated in the Codex episode — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=z1ISq9Ty4Cg — which states the ordering as a rule rather than a non-coder's workaround. **A near-identical candidate targeting `code-review` was cut**: one rule, one owner, and the ordered pass is `recheck`'s. |
| **A canonical pattern is canonical against one failure model** — outside it the pattern is not merely inert, it can amplify the real problem; record the failure it was built against and treat a mismatch as unproven | `searching-patterns` → "It generalizes *within its failure model*" | PRIMARY | Lenny's Podcast — "An inside look at X's Community Notes" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8dgyqYHLcCI . Their first production algorithm was a reputation-graph variant built against manipulation; it filtered voting rings exactly as advertised, and pilot data then showed the real problem was polarization — where one side outnumbers the other, that class of algorithm amplifies the bias. Cited to the episode, not a speaker: its own header records that neither caption track carries speaker labels. |
| **Judging a dependency, weigh usage over polish** — docs and a green suite no longer signal that anyone lived with the library; check issues from strangers answered, a changelog carrying bug fixes, real dependents | `searching-patterns` → "Weigh usage, not polish" | PRIMARY (single practitioner, self-report) | Simon Willison, Lenny's Podcast — "An AI state of the union" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wc8FBhQtdsA . He ships libraries with full docs and tests tagged *alpha*, where the tag mostly means he has not used it yet, and what he wants from other people's software is that *they* used it for months. The three checkable signals are the kit's operationalization of that, not his words. The phrase "proof of usage over proof of work" is coined in the exchange and the captions do not settle who said it, so it is not attributed. |
| **A reported bug arrives as an observation plus a diagnosis — only the observation is evidence**; re-derive severity from what was literally seen | `systematic-debugging` → "only the observation is evidence" | PRIMARY | A solo founder, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L9KvV_UOs3A . Called out of a wedding by a report that the product had been hacked in a crypto scam with user data at stake; two hours later the cause was a generated dependency whose *name* contained "crypto", and a non-technical reporter had supplied the rest of the story. |
| **Dedupe the implementation, not the assertions** — the thousand-lines-of-test-is-a-smell rule was a *maintenance* rule, and that cost moved to the agent; the engineer whose rule it was now keeps 100+ tests on a small library | `test-driven-development` → "Dedupe the implementation, not the assertions" | PRIMARY | Simon Willison, same episode — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wc8FBhQtdsA . Two limits are the kit's, not his: the tests must stay behavioral enough to throw away (a suite pinned to implementation details blocks the refactor it is meant to protect), and this governs the accumulated suite, not the one-minimal-failing-test RED step. |
| **Retiring a doc is the outcome the route-the-change list was missing** — judge on whether its purpose still holds, not on inbound links, since runbooks and reference pages are read in anger and linked by nothing | `using-compound-v` → "Retiring is the outcome nobody reaches for" | PRIMARY | Fiona Fung, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ybrl4FYM57c : a doc that was lightweight and genuinely useful was dead a few months later because the ground moved. **Corrected:** the miner substituted an inbound-link grep for the source's actual criterion (is it still serving its purpose), which would retire exactly the docs that are read and never linked. `README.md` was updated in the same edit, since it mirrors this list. |
| **Existence is not arity** — a step that reads as one thing routinely forks into per-partner or per-tenant branches, so grep call sites and sibling implementations during research, before the plan budgets one task for it | `writing-plans` → "Existence is not arity" | PRIMARY | Ryan Singer, Lenny's Podcast — "A better way to plan, build, and ship products" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GF-yUANql0c . **Corrected:** the miner's "the assumption that breaks plans most reliably" asserts a frequency the source never measures — it supplies one worked example. |
| **Rank global constraints that can pull against each other** — an unranked pair gets resolved by each batch whichever way its own tasks make cheapest, and stays green because a batch's tests assert only its own tasks | `writing-plans` → "which one wins for any two that can pull against each other" | PRIMARY (mechanism transfer) | Molly Graham, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=twzLDx9iers , rule 2 of six: one goal must win in a fight, with the MAU-inflated-by-bots example where the gameable metric loses. Transferred from company goals to plan constraints, and flagged as a transfer. |
| **Tag each core function *committed* or *under test*** — landed is a different fact from settled, and implementation is no longer expensive enough for the artifact to carry its own stage | `writing-prd` → "commitment level" | PRIMARY | Andrew Ambrosino's episode, Lenny's Podcast — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=P3KDebPTUrw : production-looking used to mean de-risked, reviewed and resourced, because you could not get the resources otherwise. The bullets carrying that passage have no speaker-settling deixis, so this is cited to the episode. The required promote-or-retire trigger reuses the skill's existing non-calendar-trigger rule. |
| **Make the goal decidable: why these users and not the adjacent ones, plus the standing trade-off and which good wins** — non-goals bound what sits outside; this resolves what sits inside | `writing-prd` → "why these users and not the adjacent ones" | PRIMARY | Kat Wu, Lenny's Podcast — "How Anthropic's product team moves faster than anyone else" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PplmzlgE0kg . The failure it prevents is an agent hitting an unresolved in-scope collision and picking silently. |
| **A capacity invalidator has a required shape** — ceiling as a quantity, current usage, growth rate, which together project the wall and let you check the lead time exceeds the re-architecture it triggers | `writing-prd` → "that threshold has a required shape" | PRIMARY | Ivan Zhao, same episode as the `make-it-stable` row — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IIPKMixTMfE . The two rows are not duplicates: that one is how to *classify a failure*, this one is what to *write in the doc*. "Plenty of room is a reassurance, not a bet" is the kit's phrasing. |
| **Cut as duplicates or already-owned, recorded so they are not re-proposed** | — | PROCESS RECORD | Five chair-level cuts from this pass: (1) *look at the output before the diff* into `code-review` — same rule as the `recheck` row above, one owner; (2) *style belongs in a committed exemplar, not the instruction file* into `context-engineering` — `batched-implementation` → "paste the exemplar" already owns it, and pass 2 had already rejected this for that reason; (3) *count the code paths under a named screen before sizing* into `extracting-specs` — same idea as the `writing-plans` "Existence is not arity" row, kept in the home where the sizing decision lives; (4) *sweep the diff for temporary scaffolding before landing* into `finishing` — same rule as the "Green says nothing about which default is live" row above; (5) *a capacity ceiling arrives as weirdness, never a status code* into `make-it-stable` — same claim and same source as the exhaustion-date row above. Four of the five are one idea proposed into two skills, which a per-candidate verifier cannot detect by construction; only a pass over the pooled result finds them. |

### Correction, same day — what a per-item gate structurally cannot see

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Duplication across workers and the resurrection of an earlier rejection both survive any per-item verifier** — a duplicate honestly passes *is this true?* twice, and its twin is a pending return rather than committed text, so no per-worker check can see it; the fix is a pooled pass at the barrier, not a stricter verifier | `dispatching-parallel-agents` → "no worker can answer it" | **MEASURED (local, this session)** | Three mining fan-outs over one corpus on 2026-08-30: per-item adversarial verifiers passed 11/17, 11/13, then 28/30. Pooling the third set lost **four to cross-worker duplication** (one idea submitted into two skills) and **one to a resurrection** of a candidate the second pass had explicitly rejected. Every verifier had been instructed to grep all 30 skills and every one answered correctly — the twins were pending, and the resurrection was absent *because* it had been rejected. **The first version of this finding blamed a softened gate and was wrong**; the mechanism is scope, not strictness, and the corrected note sits in the pass-3 block above. Sharpened in exchange with a peer session running an unrelated six-agent mining fan-out, which independently named the same shape: "the question that catches it — is this NEW? — cannot be answered from inside one item." |

| Context files: instructions help and are "well followed"; repository overviews "although popular and recommended by model providers, are not helpful", and providing the files cost **over 20% more** inference on average without generally improving resolution | `gathering-context` → "The distinction that makes this work rather than cost 20%"; `using-compound-v`; `README.md` | **PRIMARY (single study)** | Gloaguen, Mündler, Müller, Raychev et al. (ETH Zurich SRI Lab), *"Evaluating AGENTS.md: Are Repository-Level Context Files Helpful for Coding Agents?"* — https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11988 . Gloaguen, Mündler, Müller, Raychev et al., ETH Zurich SRI Lab, 2026-02-12. Both quoted fragments verified against the arXiv abstract's raw bytes, not a search digest. **One study** — carry the mechanism (task-scoped context beats a standing document) as the durable half and the 20% as one measurement, not a constant. |
| Two source counts deleted rather than defended: `recheck` claimed "seven independent sources across four lanes" and `agent-security` "(DEFAULT register — six independent sources across four lanes)" | both skills, at the lines that carried them | **REMOVED** | Neither count was enumerated anywhere in this file, and gate #8 walks ledger→file only, so an unregistered count is invisible to it by construction. A register label states how hard a claim was checked; attaching a number nobody can audit makes it a decoration that reads as rigour. The underlying claims stand — only the unbacked arithmetic went. |

| **Rejected options are recorded to CLOSE the question, not to justify the decision** — a rejection with its reason stops the same idea being relitigated | `gathering-context` → "The candidate shapes, the axis between them" | **DEFAULT** (3 independent orbits, 2 lanes) | PEP 1, python/peps @ a4f4971, `peps/pep-0001.rst`: rejected ideas recorded with reasoning, which *"[prevents] people from bringing up the same rejected idea again in subsequent discussions"* — https://peps.python.org/pep-0001/ . AWS Prescriptive Guidance, "Architectural decision record process": a rejection reason *"to prevent future discussions on the same topic"* — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/architectural-decision-records/adr-process.html . Tyree & Akerman, "Architecture Decisions: Demystifying Architecture" (IEEE Software, 2005): *"you don't want to hear the question 'Did you think about…?' during a final review"*. Three unrelated governance processes, near-identical mechanism. |
| **28% of real ADRs record the outcome and no reason** — the axis, not just the verdict, is what makes an options list usable | `gathering-context` → slot 3 | **CONTESTED** (one measurement, ours) | Measured over 387 MADR-format ADRs from 212 public repos via the GitHub code-search API: "Decision Drivers" present in 61.5%, and 71.7% of Decision Outcomes state a reason — so 28.3% state none. **This is our own measurement, not any source's claim**, and the corpus is one template's install base (`docs/decisions/` is the folder MADR's install instructions create), so it is not a sample of ADR practice in general. Carry it as an order of magnitude, never as a statistic about the field. |
| **REFUTED — "three options" is not a grounded working number** | proposed for `gathering-context` slot 3; NOT shipped | **REFUTED** | The median-3 figure replicated across a 387-ADR corpus and kubernetes/enhancements, but the ADR corpus is a single template's install base rather than an independent sample, no template or process document anywhere prescribes a count, and the k8s median was not reproducible under a stated counting rule. The skill therefore says "the alternative you did not take", never a number. Recorded so nobody re-derives it. |
| **REFUTED — "Considered Options" is not near-universal in ADR templates** | (would have justified a new options slot) | **REFUTED** | Michael Nygard's original ADR post — which coined the term — has five parts and NO alternatives field; measured over the canonical template catalogue (joelparkerhenderson/architecture-decision-record @ fb5d2f4), 6 of 11 templates name an options field and 5 do not; AWS's official minimum omits it; Backstage ships Nygard's verbatim. The alternatives field is a later, contested addition, chiefly MADR's. Any claim of consensus here would be a false citation. |
| **REMOVED — the "past successes hurt" sentence and its quotation, from `gathering-context` slot 2** | was `gathering-context` slot 2 | **REMOVED (misquoted, mis-scoped, unregistered)** | The quoted line existed only as ASR caption text and was reproduced inaccurately (two phrases dropped, two sentences fused, no ellipsis) — `scripts/yt.sh:11` and `scripts/alpha.sh` both say captions are SUBSTANCE, never QUOTATION. The source's scope is an agent re-reading its own prior trajectories in a long context window, not what prior art a person gathers before building, and its author disclaims the area on record. The row remains correctly housed at `context-engineering` → "keep failures, distrust successes" (sources.md:478), scoped to the working window and rendered as unquoted paraphrase. The effort-asymmetry argument that replaced it never depended on it. |

| **Research documents; it does not evaluate** — the research pass may not critique the implementation, propose enhancements or recommend refactors: *"documentarians, not evaluators"* | `gathering-context` → "Assembling it" | **PRIMARY** | humanlayer/humanlayer @ 99abe67, `.claude/commands/research_codebase.md`:191 (also :14, *"DO NOT critique the implementation or identify problems"*). Verified at raw bytes — a markdown file in a repo, not transcript text. The same repo's `create_plan.md` carries the option-generation step and ZERO occurrences of the documentarian prohibition: the split between enumerating and ranking is deliberate, and is why this skill enumerates while `brainstorming` picks. |
| **A finding's SHAPE is the depth, not its length** | `gathering-context` → "The shape of a finding" | **PRIMARY (one orbit, measured directly)** | vercel-labs/agent-skills @ 063bee9, `skills/react-best-practices/`. Measured at the pinned ref rather than summarised: a 948-word SKILL.md indexing 70 rule files totalling 13,606 words (a 7% index); median rule 177 words (mean 194, min 67, max 570); 64/70 carry a wrong/right pair; 65 of 69 `**Incorrect …**` labels name the defect in the LABEL while only ~20% carry a cost comment inside the code. **One orbit measured three times, not three sources** — a byte-level measurement of the named artifact, not a claim about the world. The 45%-code proportion did NOT transfer: a cold run producing 8 real task-scoped units measured a median 19.9% code, so no code-proportion bar ships. |
| **`applies` — the abstain clause** | `gathering-context` → "The shape of a finding" | **CONTESTED (one orbit)** | vercel-labs/agent-skills, `skills/vercel-optimize/references/support-topics/` — 42/42 topics carry a mandatory "Do Not Recommend When" section and gate applicability on a framework/version range. One organisation's practice, not a measured result; carried because nothing else in this kit owned "where this stops being true" and a rule without one produces confident wrong refactors on the wrong major. |
| **REFUTED — no "≤3 units per decision" cap is shipped** | proposed for `gathering-context`; NOT shipped | **REFUTED** | Two sources looked like they set the number and neither counts findings-per-decision: SkillsBench (arXiv 2602.12670) measures Skills *"with at most three modules"* — whole sections of a bundle — and vercel-optimize's `SUPPORT_TOPIC_LIMIT = 3` caps topic DOCUMENTS injected into a prompt. It also collides with this file's own earlier REFUTED row against a "three options" number. The skill says "few, and say which you dropped" instead. |
| **REMOVED — three unsourced quotations in `gathering-context`'s four derivative-work rules** | was `gathering-context` → "Four rules that keep prior art from making the work derivative" | **REMOVED (unregistered, unlandable)** | *"abstractions freeze assumptions… your abstractions become constraints"*, *"because that is the natural tendency"*, and *"that's some engineer that doesn't work here anymore"* each carried a quoted attribution and NONE had a row in this file — invisible to gate #8, which walks ledger→file and cannot see a quote that was never entered. Almost certainly transcript text, which `scripts/yt.sh` forbids quoting. All four rules survive their removal intact. |

| **The ladder of scope: prompt → context → harness → loop are one practice at four units of concern, not four subjects** | `gathering-context` opening; `references/context-is-the-work.md`; README "The premise" | **CONDITIONAL** | Anthropic Applied AI, "Effective context engineering for AI agents", 29 Sep 2025 — https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/effective-context-engineering-for-ai-agents : *"we view context engineering as the natural progression of prompt engineering"*, context defined as *"the set of tokens included when sampling"*. Karpathy, 25 Jun 2025 — https://x.com/karpathy/status/1937902205765607626 . **The two are ONE EVENT with two bylines** — Karpathy was amplifying Tobi Lütke days earlier — so this is 2 independent origins, not 3. CONDITIONAL because the reduction is a SCOPE claim, not an identity claim: stated as identity it is refuted (see the counterexample row below). "Harness engineering" is the fourth rung and the one this kit occupies; `sources.md:401` already cites it. |
| **Three things on that ladder are NOT context** — the counterexample that keeps the thesis from being a slogan | `references/context-is-the-work.md` → "Where the ladder is not about context" | **DEFAULT** | (a) Decoding-time levers — self-consistency sampling, logit masking — change output with a byte-identical token set; this kit's own `context-engineering` skill prescribes one. (b) Loop engineering's working definition is a list of BOUNDS: a machine-checkable stop condition, a cost ceiling, a permission boundary, an escalation policy, and verifier/implementer independence — plus worktree isolation, which is mutual exclusion between writers, not information. Term coined by Addy Osmani, 7 Jun 2026 — https://addyosmani.com/blog/loop-engineering/ : *"Loop engineering is replacing yourself as the person who prompts the agent. You design the system that does it instead."* (c) The field's own taxonomy splits control from information: the prompt says what to DO, the context supplies what to KNOW. |
| **The operating verb is MINIMISATION, not supply — too much context measurably degrades** | `references/context-is-the-work.md`; README | **DEFAULT** | Anthropic (same post): context rot means *"as the number of tokens in the context window increases, the model's ability to accurately recall information from that context decreases"* and *"this characteristic emerges across all models"*; context is *"a finite resource with diminishing marginal returns"*; the goal is *"finding the smallest possible set of high-signal tokens"*. Karpathy states both directions in the coining post: *"Too much or too irrelevant and the LLM costs might go up and performance might come down."* Plus arXiv 2602.11988 (repository context files: no general improvement, 20%+ added cost). |
| **"Mistakes are MOSTLY a lack of context" — the QUANTIFIER is refuted; the AXIS replaces it** | `references/context-is-the-work.md` → "Why missing context produces error" | **REFUTED as stated / CONDITIONAL as scoped** | Replace "mostly" with: when the missing thing is **retrievable evidence**, context sufficiency dominates the outcome; when it is the **inferential or executional step over evidence already present**, more context does nothing. Supporting the first half: Joren et al. (Google, ICLR 2025) — with insufficient context, models hallucinate rather than abstain **15.4–40.4%** of the time; Agentless (Xia, Deng, Dunn, Zhang, UIUC) hand-classified all 300 SWE-bench Lite problems and found **10.0%** lack enough information to be solvable at all. |
| **REFUTED — do not cite Dunning-Kruger for "too little context to see your own error"** | proposed for `references/context-is-the-work.md`; NOT shipped as a citation | **REFUTED** | The canonical quartile chart plots (y−x) against x, so it reproduces the crossing lines from random numbers; the causal claim — that missing knowledge is itself what hides the error — has been disputed in print since 2002 and is unresolved. Direct counter-evidence: tournament chess players receiving objective, precise, public feedback on their exact skill for years still rated themselves ~89 Elo above their real rating, so overconfidence is not only a missing-context problem. Also do NOT cite the DK-in-LLM papers: the strongest compares four models and rests on two endpoints. |
| **REFUTED — the Palantir attribution for "the best AI systems are really just excellent software"** | was proposed for the README/philosophy | **REFUTED (attribution); DEFAULT (idea)** | 136 @palantirdevelopers titles enumerated live and 13 talks fully transcribed, including the CTO's and every DevCon opening keynote: no version of the statement. Drop the attribution rather than soften it. The landable source is **Dex Horthy**, humanlayer/12-factor-agents @ d20c728 README — verified verbatim at raw bytes: most products billing themselves as "AI Agents" are *"not all that agentic"*, *"mostly deterministic code, with LLM steps sprinkled in at just the right points"*, and *"Agents, at least the good ones… are comprised of mostly just software."* |
| **"Revolutionary systems are built by synthesising patterns from several projects"** | `references/context-is-the-work.md` → "Synthesis across projects" | **CONTESTED (one first-person account)** | The account is the kit author's own — he reports this is how he has worked across his projects. One source, carried labelled, with his position inside the sentence: he is also the person whose kit is built on it. A sweep found **no published** first-person account across ~2,900 enumerated titles and 16 transcripts, and no measurement either way — but that silence is weak evidence, and the mechanism is one this kit already carries: practitioners publish the RESULT, not the assembly method, because naming the seams reads as derivative while doing it reads as judgment. Treat the zero as **unmeasured, not refuted**. Falsifier: if teams that survey prior art before designing produce measurably MORE derivative work than teams that do not, the claim fails in the direction that matters. Moves to DEFAULT on two or three unrelated first-person accounts naming the specific patterns lifted and their sources. |
| **NARROWED — "the corpus substitutes for a CTO's accumulated experience"** | `gathering-context`; `references/context-is-the-work.md` | **CONDITIONAL** | The PREMISE survives and is conceded even by the thesis's sharpest critics: experiential context is the binding thing and cannot be written down in advance. The REMEDY does not survive at full strength. This kit's own earlier pass over eight primary essays reached the same place independently — **port the method that generates the answer, never the settled answer.** |
| **Observation: "context engineering" is a vendor-lane term** | `references/context-is-the-work.md` closing | **CONTESTED (one measurement, ours)** | In a sweep of all 32 verified channels, every "context engineering" hit came from a vendor or tooling channel (cloud providers, framework vendors, course platforms) and **zero** from a research-lab channel. Our own measurement, not a source's claim. It does not make the idea wrong — it is a reason to hold the frame loosely and to notice that the research lane locates capability elsewhere. |

| **A green suite is not proof — you have to run it yourself** | `verification-before-completion` → "Why a green suite is not proof"; `test-driven-development` → "Why RED is a separate step" | **PRIMARY** | Simon Willison, "Hallucinations in code are the least dangerous form of LLM mistakes", 2 Mar 2025 — https://simonwillison.net/2025/Mar/2/hallucinations-in-code/ . Verified verbatim at raw bytes: *"No amount of meticulous code review—or even comprehensive automated tests—will demonstrably prove that code actually does the right thing. You have to run it yourself!"* and *"you need to actively exercise that code. You need to have great manual QA skills… never trust any piece of code until you've seen it work with your own eye—or, even better, seen it fail and then fixed it."* The last clause is an independent statement of the red-first rule from someone not arguing for TDD, which is why it carries weight there. **Note:** an earlier draft cited this to the wrong Willison post; the URL above is the one the strings actually appear in. |
| **Five bounds an unattended run owes you: stop condition · cost ceiling · permission boundary · escalation policy · verifier/implementer independence** | `handoff` → "What an unattended run owes you" | **DEFAULT** | Loop engineering's consolidated working definition (Lulla, Nersesyan, Mohsenimofidi, Treude & Baltes, arXiv 2608.21884), plus Addy Osmani, who named the practice 7 Jun 2026 — https://addyosmani.com/blog/loop-engineering/ , verified at raw bytes: *"Loop engineering is replacing yourself as the person who prompts the agent. You design the system that does it instead."* Osmani's own list adds git worktrees for parallel isolation. **Load-bearing observation carried into the skill:** none of the five is information you could put in a context window — this is the part of agent work that gathering more context cannot help with, which is the counterexample to this kit's own premise and the reason it is written down. |
| **What the application layer is for: private data, sensors, actuators, feedback loops** | `architecting-ai-systems` → "What the app layer is for" | **CONTESTED (one source, his own blog)** | Andrej Karpathy, https://karpathy.bearblog.dev/year-in-review-2025/ — verified verbatim: *"LLM labs will trend to graduate the generally capable college student, but LLM apps will organize, finetune and actually animate teams of them into deployed professionals in specific verticals by supplying private data, sensors and actuators and feedback loops."* Carried with the word **finetune** left inside the quote deliberately: even the strongest statement of the app-layer thesis does not claim context alone does it. |
| **Prefer giving the agent the ABILITY TO FETCH over pasting the content** | `gathering-context` → "The shape of a finding" | **CONTESTED (one source)** | Trenton Bricken (mechanistic interpretability, Anthropic), Dwarkesh Patel written transcript — https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/sholto-trenton-2 , verified at raw bytes: *"My question is always, are you giving the model enough context? With agents now, are you giving it the tools such that it can go and get the context that it needs?"* A written transcript, not captions, so quotable. One source, and he works at the lab whose harness this kit runs on. |
| **The boundary on the whole premise: experiential context cannot be put in in advance** | `gathering-context` → "Stop" | **PRIMARY (a critic's concession)** | Richard Sutton, Dwarkesh Patel written transcript — https://www.dwarkesh.com/p/richard-sutton , verified at raw bytes: the reason people become useful on a job is that they encounter their particular part of the world — *"It can't have been anticipated and can't all have been put in in advance. The world is so huge that you can't."* Carried because it comes from the thesis's sharpest critic and marks where a pack stops substituting for experience: when the slots are full and the thing is still underdetermined, that is the boundary, not a failure to gather harder. |
| **The cost hierarchy: a bad line of research costs more than a bad line of code** | `gathering-context` → "The distinction that makes this work" | **DEFAULT** | Will Larson (repeat CTO — Carta, Calm, Stripe, Uber), "Make no assumptions." — https://lethain.com/make-no-assumptions/ , verified verbatim at raw bytes: *"once any reasoning layer is poisoned, it's impossible to reason effectively on top of it"*, in a first-person account where accepting an initial analysis would have led him to push a team onto a project solving an illusionary problem. Corroborated across two further lanes. Larson's is the strongest instance because it is a negative result about his own analysis with nothing to sell. |

## Context/research lane — vendor retraction + always-on cost (2026-09-07)

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Vendor documentation is retracted IN PLACE, under the same URL** — the citation still resolves while the recommendation has expired | `searching-patterns` → "check whether the vendor has retracted it in place" | **PRIMARY (both pages fetched live 2026-09-07)** | Anthropic, "The think tool" — https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/claude-think-tool now opens under **Extended thinking update, Dec 15, 2025** with *"Extended thinking capabilities have improved since its initial release, such that we recommend using that feature instead of a dedicated think tool in most cases."* Anthropic, "Building effective agents" — https://www.anthropic.com/research/building-effective-agents now opens with *"Much of the tooling landscape described in this post has changed since December 2024. For our current approach, see how we built Claude Managed Agents and the Managed Agents documentation."* Fetched this session rather than recalled, which is the rule the row states. The kit's own exposure was counted, not assumed: `grep -c building-effective-agents references/sources.md` = **7** — five claim rows (`designing-agents` ×2, `batched-implementation`, `simplest-thing-that-works`, `make-it-stable`), the primary-source index, and this row itself (a count of a file that names the file counts its own line — say the number the command returns, not the number you meant) — and every one rests on the workflow-taxonomy / ACI half the banner does not touch. |
| **The retraction is PARTIAL: the tooling half turned over, the taxonomy did not** | `searching-patterns` → "the tooling half the banner names has been rewritten under it" | **PRIMARY (verified live 2026-09-07)** | Live framework list on that page: **Claude Agent SDK, Strands Agents SDK (AWS), Rivet, Vellum.** Read off the fetched page. **No claim is made about the December 2024 original's list** — it was not re-fetched, so "it used to say LangGraph and Bedrock" is deliberately absent rather than repeated. The transferable half is that a partial retraction keeps the page's authority while half of it expires, so spot-checking the part you remember confirms the page and carries the dead part with it. |
| **A standing ruleset's net cost flips SIGN by model class** — 42–75% cheaper on three Claude models, 26–39% *more* expensive on two OpenAI reasoning models | `references/shapes.md` → "the frozen content is the other half of the bill" | **PRIMARY (first-party — the ruleset's author benchmarking his own ruleset), disclosed AGAINST interest** | DietrichGebert/ponytail — https://github.com/DietrichGebert/ponytail , `benchmarks/results/2026-06-17-cost-verification.md` as reported in the mining pass (verified at the raw bytes of a local clone; every figure in this row reproduces). Pooled over **30 reps** per Claude model: Haiku **63.1%** cheaper, Sonnet **74.5%**, Opus **42.3%**. Over **10 reps** on OpenAI: gpt-4.1-mini **39.6%** cheaper, gpt-5.4-mini **26.2% MORE** expensive, gpt-5.5 **38.7% MORE** expensive at **0.9×** latency (slower). README:93: *"a terse reasoning model that spends thinking tokens deliberating the rungs can go the other way (on GPT-5.5 it does)."* A vendor's numbers about its own product are marketing — except where they contradict the pitch, which these do (the pitch is that the ruleset saves money). Carry the **sign flip** as the durable half and the decimals as one harness on one date. |
| **Automatic prompt caching ZEROES the cost telemetry on repeated identical prompts** | `references/shapes.md` → "returned the repeats as `cached`" | **PRIMARY (first-party, same repo)** | Same results file: runs 2 and 3 *"could not be pooled because OpenAI's automatic prompt caching collapsed the token telemetry on identical repeated prompts (reported as `cached`, with `prompt`/`completion`/`cost` zeroed)."* Generalises past that one provider by mechanism: a repeated-prompt harness is precisely the workload prefix caching is built for, so the repetitions you add for statistical power are the ones whose cost fields come back empty. Verify the telemetry before pooling repeats. |
| **An ambient activation mechanism contaminates its own control arm — and the bias UNDERSTATES** | `evals` → "a control that also holds the treatment shrinks every difference toward zero"; `references/context-is-the-work.md` → "an ambient mechanism contaminates every measurement of itself" | **PRIMARY (first-party, disclosed against interest)** | DietrichGebert/ponytail, `benchmarks/agentic/README.md` as reported: the earlier agentic-safety run (the **~4%** gap) is *"superseded"* — *"its baseline was contaminated by the ponytail plugin's `SessionStart` hook firing on every arm, so the baseline was secretly running ponytail. Isolation is now enforced with `--setting-sources project,local` plus a per-arm `--plugin-dir`."* The isolated re-run's headline is **−54%** mean LOC (12 tasks, Haiku 4.5, n=4). **The two figures measure different things and NO correction factor is claimed** — what is inferable from the mechanism alone is the sign: a control that also holds the treatment shrinks every between-arm difference toward zero, so an unimpressive result is exactly what a leaked control produces. |
| **The platform's own generator is the DELETE nobody looks for** | `references/prior-art.md` → "Run the platform's own generator" | **CONTESTED (single practitioner account, not publicly landable)** | Russian-language Telegram channel on AI coding, message mined 2026-09-07: the practitioner generated an entire editor plugin, learned mid-build that the platform ships an official project template, and rewrote the finished functionality onto it. One instance, one platform, no control arm. It ships as an **operation** (run the vendor's `create-*`/`init` into a scratch dir before writing a line; the diff is the finding) rather than as the anecdote, because the operation costs one command and is checkable by anyone, which is the bar a single-source row has to clear to be actionable. A channel message is not a URL, and this row says so rather than dressing it as one. |

## Chair-pooled lane — practitioner corpus, SDLC talk, and harness reference (2026-09-09)

| Claim (short) | Anchor | Category | Source / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The compaction spiral's cause is the shape of the code, not the size of the window** — every rung of the compaction ladder is an in-window lever and none of them reaches lost locality | `context-engineering` → "short of *locality*, not of window" | **CONTESTED (single source; ASR transcript — paraphrased, never quoted)** | Conference talk on why AI adoption in the SDLC fails (46 min, 2026-07-29), [00:21:47]–[00:23:06]: the team dropped its own engineering practices on the reasoning that best practices had been written for developers and agents do not care about them; locality was lost, working out one task meant gathering modules from several places, and the window began overflowing and compacting in a loop. A machine transcript is substance, never quotation. The talk offers no number and none is used — its only quantitative phrase is "genuinely speeds something up," which is not a measurement. The trap carried in the skill (*indirection is not locality*) is this kit's addition, not the talk's. |
| **An ARCHITECTURE_CONCERN indicts one of two things** — the plan, or the code that existed before the task and cannot host it — and only the second's fix is a preparatory change booked as its own task | `recheck` → "Name which of two things it indicts" | **CONTESTED (one practitioner; the second cause has no second source)** | Russian-language Telegram channel on AI coding, message #151 (2025-10-06), mined 2026-09-09, verbatim: «Если есть какие то ошибки или неточности, то это проблемы стадии планирования… Либо, текущий код, который был до этой задачи не позволяет имплементировать то, что было задумано.» [if there are errors or inaccuracies, those are problems of the planning stage… or the code that existed before this task does not allow implementing what was conceived]. The FIRST cause has a second, second-hand source (#232, 2026-02-01) and was already the kit's rule, so it is not what the edit adds. The second cause is one voice, which is why the skill ships it as a **naming requirement** rather than a diagnosis: unnamed it is unfalsifiable, and an unfalsifiable second cause licenses a refactor nobody asked for. A channel message is not a URL, and this row says so. |
| **A document written for a future agent is verified by a contextless subagent executing against it** — no questions answered, outcome graded rather than transcript | `verification-before-completion` → "verified by making one use it, not by reading it back" | **CONTESTED (single practitioner; machine-transcribed captions)** | Same talk, [00:44:22]–[00:45:51], mined 2026-09-09. **ASR captions — substance, never quotation**, so paraphrased: asked how to write the manual when the agent's current context may hold distortions (you tell it to save the session as a skill, it saves, and it still misses the important things), the speaker's answer is to launch a subagent with no context, let it solve the original task using only that skill while you watch, and treat a wrong solve as the skill's missing paragraph. No number attached and none claimed. The kit's own statement of the problem it answers is `writing-plans` → *"a reviewer of prose has no ground truth to appeal to."* |
| **A schedule is a standing grant** — a cron'd or thread-bound agent re-arms from the clock after every kill, so a turn cap and a stop-it-mid-run, both scoped to a run, do not bind it; revoke at the scheduler and the credential | `agent-security` → "A schedule is a standing grant" | **JUDGMENT-CALL / CANONICAL** | Follows from what a scheduler is: run N+1 is started by the clock and not by the session you stopped, so a control scoped to a run cannot reach it. The trifecta consequence is this skill's own mechanism 2 read against a run with no human in it — the clause that says "the price is a human in the loop" has no one to charge. Prompted by two field recipes that ship this shape with no security consideration at all (a weekly cron'd repo-sweep agent holding git plus tracker write; a 6-hourly heartbeat holding production ssh at full access), both from a practitioner's private channel with **no public URL** — so the rule ships on its own reasoning and the recipes are the motivation, not the evidence. Do not quote a number from them. Scope check: `sources.md` REFUSED PHASES clause (j) assigns blast-radius provisioning to this skill, and clauses (a)/(b)/(i) refuse the *operate stage* those recipes came wrapped in — that refusal stands. |
| **A suppression buys the same false green as a weakened assertion** — `as any`, `@ts-ignore`, `# type: ignore`, `# noqa` and `eslint-disable` make the checker exit 0 without the property holding, and the review pass is told to skip exactly what a checker would have caught | `test-driven-development` → "The type error is noise" | **JUDGMENT-CALL / CANONICAL** | Follows from what a suppression comment does, composed with `code-review`'s stated scope exclusion ("Anything a linter / type-checker / compiler / CI would catch — imports, types, formatting, broken tests. A practitioner's field rule — configure linters for `as any` and dead code rather than style, because nobody reads the code round the clock any more — prompted it and carries **no public URL**. ## AI Engineer channel + agentic-systems pass (2026-09-21)

*All 1,159 talks listed on the AI Engineer channel on 2026-09-21 were triaged view-blind (popularity was withheld from
the triagers), 67 were read in full, and the reading list is now `references/talks.tsv`. Alongside
them: eight open-source repos read at pinned refs, five X cohorts of founders/CTOs via a read API,
nine lanes over the local agentic-systems corpus, and the harness's own current documentation. 798
findings pooled; each row below survived three adversarial lenses (duplication, source fidelity,
value). What did not survive is held out of the published kit, with its reason. Talks are ASR:
substance, never quotation, unless the row quotes a written source.*

| A third pass over an unchanged failure is the shape that eats whole runs: agents circle, making small variations to one broken approach | `systematic-debugging` → "small variations to the same broken approach" | PRIMARY (mechanism; one vendor write-up) | LangChain, "Improving Deep Agents with harness engineering" — https://www.langchain.com/blog/improving-deep-agents-with-harness-engineering — verbatim, fetched 2026-09-21: "Agents can be myopic once they've decided on a plan which results in 'doom loops' that make small variations to the same broken approach (10+ times in some traces)." Replaces the UNSOURCED "~20 of 89" count (row "One team bucketing 89 agent tasks…"): that source states no count, and Cline's talk and its hill-climbing blog state none either. Do not substitute SWE-agent's 90.5% → 57.2% (arXiv 2405.15793 §5.2): it is recovery after a failed *edit action* (a lint error), not after a failed fix hypothesis. |
| "That failure is pre-existing" is a claim with a one-command test (stash the change or check out the base, run it); deep in a long session it precedes hack fixes | `systematic-debugging` → "code-review's revert test, turned on yourself" | CONTESTED (one practitioner, talk) | Dex Horthy (HumanLayer), "The Great Loops Debate" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c35YoMdnI78 — [talk, ASR: paraphrase only] about 200k tokens in, a model trying to get tests passing reaches for odd hacks and its thinking dismisses the failing test as from something else, pre-existing, when it is not; he names this as the telltale of a degraded session. The operation is the kit's own `code-review` revert test ("would this still be broken if the diff were reverted?"). Falsifier: a sample of agent "pre-existing" claims that, checked against the base, are nearly all true — then the row costs a command and catches nothing. |
| A plan carrying real code runs about as long as its diff, so a plan earns its keep by settling decisions before code depends on them, not by being a shorter read | `writing-plans` → "not because it is shorter than its diff" | CONTESTED (one practitioner reversing his own written claim) + internal consistency — NARROWED 2026-09-29: the two rules it cites were removed (see the SUPERSEDED row) | The kit's own rules force it: "Code in the plan is source" and the no-placeholder rule ("Similar to Task N — repeat the code") make a plan carry every line it produces. The replaced premise ("a 200-line plan … the 2,000-line diff") traces to Dex Horthy's August 2025 essay — https://github.com/humanlayer/advanced-context-engineering-for-coding-agents/blob/18608de/ace-fca.md — verbatim: "I can't read 2000 lines of golang daily. But I *can* read 200 lines of a well-written implementation plan." He reversed it in "Everything We Got Wrong About RPI" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YwZR6tc7qYg — [talk, ASR: paraphrase] a thousand-line plan tends to produce about a thousand lines of code, within roughly 10%. The 200-line cap itself stays a JUDGMENT-CALL sized for the reader. Do not answer this by adding a separate design or outline document: REFUSED PHASES (d). |
| When a feature crosses layers, order the plan by slice, not by layer: models default to stack order (migrations, services, API, UI), so nothing runs until the last task, and naming the rule does not remove the default | `writing-plans` → "cut its tasks as slices, not layers" | CONTESTED (one written load-bearing source for the model default; a second practitioner corroborates in a talk) | Dex Horthy, wsff.md @4aa70bc — https://github.com/humanlayer/advanced-context-engineering-for-coding-agents/blob/4aa70bc/wsff.md — verbatim: "Models love what I call \"horizontal plans\" - doing things in stack-order:" … "1. Create API contract and serve mock data, test with curl" / "2. Create frontend to consume mock data, iterate+polish in browser" … "Most frontier models won't design a plan like this without human steering". HumanLayer's own earlier create_plan.md @99abe67 prescribed the horizontal order ("Start with data model" … "Implement UI last"). Matt Pocock, "Full Walkthrough: Workflow for AI Coding" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-QFHIoCo-Ko — [talk, ASR: paraphrase] with vertical slices written into his skill, the model's first proposed slice was still a service with no UI; he rejected it by hand. Dex and Pocock discussed the idea together (Jan 2026 livestream), so count them as related, not independent. The principle predates AI (Shape Up, "Integrate one slice", linked from wsff.md; not re-read in this pass). Falsifier: an unsteered plan on a current model whose tasks already come out vertical. |
| CUT from `writing-plans`: "models improve until regenerating from a good spec beats maintaining the output … treat the instructions as the durable asset, worth more than the code they produce", with the duplicate PRD pointer it sat under | — | REMOVED (unsourced; contradicted) | No ledger row ever grounded it. The practitioners who ran spec-regeneration as a workflow report the opposite: Matt Pocock, "Full Walkthrough: Workflow for AI Coding" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-QFHIoCo-Ko — [talk, ASR: paraphrase] he really tried edit-the-spec-regenerate-the-code and it does not work, because the code is where the shaping happens; Dex Horthy, "Everything We Got Wrong About RPI" — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YwZR6tc7qYg — [talk, ASR] months of not reading the code ended with large parts ripped out. The PRD pointer duplicated the skill's own "Producing the product's stable PRD instead?" line. Do not reinstate without a first-person source that ran regeneration as a workflow. |

| **A turn that ends with agent work still in flight is paused, not stopped — the ledger gate abstains** while the Stop input's `background_tasks` holds a subagent or workflow task — never a teammate or a backgrounded shell, which report `running` for the whole session and would disarm the gate for the rest of the run, and still fires when only a backgrounded shell is running | `hooks/stop-ledger` → "PAUSED IS NOT STOPPED"; pinned in `scripts/hooks-test.sh` | **PRIMARY / verifiable (vendor docs = mechanism) + reproduced** | Claude Code hooks reference, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks (Stop input, fetched 2026-09-21): *"The `background_tasks` and `session_crons` arrays let hooks distinguish "session is done" from "session is paused waiting for background work to wake it back up"."* Sub-agents reference, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents: fork mode is on by default in an interactive session, every subagent runs in the background, and *"Claude can't ask for the foreground."* /goal, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/goal: *"If a subagent or a background shell command is still running when a turn ends, Claude Code skips the evaluation for that turn."* Invisible to the kit's instruments: fork mode is off under `claude -p`, which trigger-eval.sh and arm.sh use. Deliberately narrower than /goal: shells are excluded because a backgrounded dev server would disarm the gate for a whole GSD run. The subagent case in hooks-test.sh fails on the unpatched hook. |
| **The completion claim is read from `last_assistant_message`, not the transcript tail** — the transcript is written asynchronously and can end one message early at Stop time, and a fail-open gate reading it then misses exactly the claim it exists to catch, with no signal | `hooks/stop-verify` → "last_assistant_message"; pinned in `scripts/hooks-test.sh` | **PRIMARY / verifiable (vendor docs) + reproduced** | Claude Code hooks reference, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, Stop input (fetched 2026-09-21): *"use this field rather than reading `transcript_path`: the transcript file isn't guaranteed to include the final message at Stop time on all versions."* Two hooks-test.sh cases, both failing on the unpatched hook: a lagging tail whose real final message is a claim, and a flushed claim that the real final message withdraws. Residual: if the lag also hides the turn's last Bash call, the gate can false-block once, bounded by `stop_hook_active`. That is less likely than the miss being fixed, because tool calls are written seconds before the final message. |

| Agentic search (a model writing the queries): start lexical (BM25 + grep), tune BM25 to document length, check a benchmark's losing arm before it picks the retriever; embeddings for paraphrase; retrieve-into-workspace-then-grep past shell scale | `architecting-ai-systems` → "Agentic search — a model writing the queries" | **CONDITIONAL** (axis: identifier-dense queries vs same meaning in different words; corpus size) | Hsu, Yang & Lin, "Rethinking Agentic Search with Pi-Serini: Is Lexical Retrieval Sufficient?" arXiv 2605.10848: *"Tuning raises accuracy from 64.0% to 82.0%"* (k1=25, b=1 against Anserini's default k1=0.9, b=0.4); abstract: *"Pi-Serini with gpt-5.5 achieves 83.1% answer accuracy and 94.7% surfaced evidence recall, outperforming released search agents that use dense retrievers."* The comparison it revisits: BrowseComp-Plus, arXiv 2508.06600 — GPT-5 55.9% with BM25 vs *"70.1% with fewer search calls"* with Qwen3-Embedding-8B. DR-DCI, arXiv 2606.14885: *"In corpus-scaling experiments, DR-DCI remains effective from 100K to 10M documents, whereas raw DCI becomes unstable and BM25 performs substantially worse."* The three papers share one academic orbit (Waterloo) — count them as one. Independent: Eugene Yan — https://eugeneyan.com/writing/llm-patterns/: *"I’ve found that hybrid retrieval (traditional search index + embedding-based search) works better than either alone."*, embeddings failing on names, acronyms and IDs, keyword search on synonyms and hypernyms. Vendor framings not relied on: Hornet (sells BM25) and Cursor / turbopuffer (sell semantic search). |

Omar Khattab — https://x.com/lateinteraction/status/2082689553626701839: *"And a separate notion of a harness that’s last-mile specification over the underlying bland model. This is irreducible to the extent that your task/context diverges from the mode, regardless of capability."* |
| Choose between candidate architectures by the gain from a weak→strong swap within one model family, not by one model's score | `architecting-ai-systems` → "Choose between architectures by what a stronger model does to them" | **CONTESTED** (one source; falsifier: a design that wins the weak→strong delta yet loses on the strong model's absolute score for the task you ship) | Yichao 'Peak' Ji (Manus), X 2025-07-23 — https://x.com/peakji/status/1948060791636410404: *"At Manus, we never commit to an architecture based on static benchmarks. Instead, we treat models (M) as external variables and evaluate agent architectures (F) using two deltas:"* … *"1. Maximize F(M_strong) - F(M_weak) within the same model family"* … *"We prioritize (1) over (2), and only consider (2) when models are agent-aligned."* Follow-up — https://x.com/peakji/status/1948235806394769758: *"Choose the system architecture that benefits the most from switching to a stronger model, instead of comparing design choices using the same model."* Same direction from a second orbit, talk only: Ara Khan (Cline) — a very capable model can make a poor harness score well. Operationalises the skill's own opening claim that a good shape lets a better base model help you. |
| The 10–30% band has a named dissent: aim at 0–1% and read 20% as a warning — the axis is whether your edge is something the next general model cannot supply | `architecting-ai-systems` → "The band has a named dissent" | **CONDITIONAL** (the kit's METR-derived inference vs one practitioner; axis named) | Jeff Dean (Google), YC fireside "Jeff Dean: The 1% Rule for Building in AI" (2026-07-30) — https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CxXgV54KzpQ [talk; substance, not quotation]: test current general models in your domain; complete failure is probably a good sign, partial success is not, because the capability is starting to be present and more data or scale will improve it; look for problems where the model succeeds 0% or 1%, not 20%; the two winning shapes are a product with data the general model cannot see and a niche model trained on the right data. The other side stays the kit's own inference from METR (row "the 10–30% success band"), whose practitioner form is building for the next model ("product overhang"). |

Anthropic's doc-coauthoring skill @ 34040c9: *"For each question, invoke a sub-agent with just the document content and the question."* Thorsten Ball (Amp), https://x.com/thorstenball/status/2097184659150889267 (2026-09-08): after each `AGENTS.md` change an agent *"spawns 3 new orbs and tells them to do a certain task (without mentioning the change we made)"* and reads their threads — judged from the trajectory, not the report. Scoped to one-way doors because it costs a full execution. |

| At `high`+, **verify each surviving finding in a fresh subagent that never sees the finder's reasoning**, presumes it false, and keeps it only with evidence; a finder that also verifies drops true positives, a verifier that reads the reasoning agrees | `code-review` → "verify each survivor outside the pass that found it" | DEFAULT (four orbits: code, written, talk) | Anthropic claude-code plugin, commit 9babb8dbbf (2025-12-06, "Update the code review prompt") deleted the Haiku 0–100 scorer and "Filter out any issues with a score less than 80" and added: "For each issue found in the previous step by agents 3 and 4, launch parallel subagents to validate the issue. These subagents should get the PR title and description along with a description of the issue." — unchanged at HEAD db8834ba1d (2026-03-12). Anthropic, "Using LLMs to secure source code", https://claude.com/blog/using-llms-to-secure-source-code: "When an agent tries to do both in the same step, it can self censor and exclude exploitable true positives." / "If the verifier is exposed to the discovery agent’s reasoning, it may simply agree instead of testing the claim." / "adding an adversarial verifier roughly halved the rate of non-exploitable findings" (one orbit with the plugin). Cloudflare, "Project Glasswing: what Mythos showed us", https://blog.cloudflare.com/cyber-frontier-models/ (a Glasswing partner — adjacent orbit, own harness): "one with a different prompt, a different model, and no ability to generate its own findings - catches a lot of the noise that the first agent would miss if it just checked its own work." Unregistered until this row.
